Step 2, distributing alloc and size literals

In this step, we aim at showing that

\vdash_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathsf{C}}(\ast)}\textit{\small{Alloc}}\ast\textit{\small{Garb}}\Rightarrow\varphi^{(1)}\ast\psi^{(1)}

where
\varphi^{(1)}
and
\psi^{(1)}
are two formulae defined as follows:

\varphi^{(1)}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mbox{\tiny def}}}{{=}}\begin{cases}\mathtt{size}=\max_{\mathtt{size}}(\varphi)\land\bigwedge\{\mathtt{alloc}(\mathtt{x}_{i})\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\varphi\mid i\in[1,n]\}&\text{if}~\max_{\mathtt{size}}(\varphi)<\alpha\\[3.0pt]
\mathtt{size}\geq\max_{\mathtt{size}}(\varphi)\land\bigwedge\{\mathtt{alloc}(\mathtt{x}_{i})\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\varphi\mid i\in[1,n]\}&\text{otherwise}\end{cases}

\psi^{(1)}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mbox{\tiny def}}}{{=}}\begin{cases}\mathtt{size}=\max_{\mathtt{size}}(\psi)\land\bigwedge\{\mathtt{alloc}(\mathtt{x}_{i})\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\psi\mid i\in[1,n]\}&\text{if}~\max_{\mathtt{size}}(\psi)<\alpha\\[3.0pt]
\mathtt{size}\geq\max_{\mathtt{size}}(\psi)\land\bigwedge\{\mathtt{alloc}(\mathtt{x}_{i})\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\psi\mid i\in[1,n]\}&\text{otherwise}\end{cases}

We use the notations
\varphi^{(1)}
and
\psi^{(1)}
since later in the proof,
we shall consider sequences of formulae
\varphi^{(1)},\ldots,\varphi^{(k)}

and
\psi^{(1)},\ldots,\psi^{(k)}
with increasing amount of literals.
That is why, using
\varphi^{(1)}
and
\psi^{(1)}
at this early stage is meaningful.
Before tackling this derivation, a few more steps are required.
First of all, notice that,
if there is a formula
\mathtt{alloc}(\mathtt{x})
occurring both in
\varphi
and
\psi
,
then, by definition of
{\langle\mbox{$\ast$}\rangle}(\varphi,\psi)
,

\mathtt{x}\neq\mathtt{x}
occurs in
{\langle\mbox{$\ast$}\rangle}(\varphi,\psi)
.
This contradicts the fact that
{\langle\mbox{$\ast$}\rangle}(\varphi,\psi)
is satisfiable.
Therefore, we derive that the set of variables
\mathtt{x}_{1},\dots,\mathtt{x}_{n}
can be split into
two disjoint subsets, the ones “allocated”
in
\varphi
, and the others in
\psi
. Let
n_{\varphi}
(resp.
n_{\psi}
) denote the number
of equivalence classes of variables allocated in
\varphi
(resp.
\psi
).
Clearly,
n=n_{\varphi}+n_{\psi}
.
Moreover, since
\varphi
and
\psi
are satisfiable core types in
\mathsf{CoreTypes}(\mathtt{X},\alpha)
, where
\alpha\geq{\rm card}(\mathtt{X})
,
we must have
n_{\varphi}\leq\max_{\mathtt{size}}(\varphi)
and
n_{\psi}\leq\max_{\mathtt{size}}(\psi)
(see
the axiom
(

I
C

11

)
).
By
A.
, we conclude that
n\leq\max_{\mathtt{size}}({\langle\mbox{$\ast$}\rangle}(\varphi,\psi))
.
We define the following formulae

\displaystyle\textit{\small{Alloc}}(\varphi)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mbox{\tiny def}}}{{=}}\Asterisk\{\mathtt{alloc}(\mathtt{x}_{i})\wedge\mathtt{size}=1\mid\mathtt{alloc}(\mathtt{x}_{i})\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\varphi,\,i\in[1,n]\}

\displaystyle\textit{\small{Garb}}(\varphi)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mbox{\tiny def}}}{{=}}\begin{cases}\mathtt{size}=\max_{\mathtt{size}}(\varphi)-n_{\varphi}&\text{if}~\max_{\mathtt{size}}(\varphi)\,{<}\,\alpha\\[2.0pt]
\mathtt{size}\geq\max_{\mathtt{size}}(\varphi)-n_{\varphi}&\text{otherwise}\end{cases}

Notice that, since
\max_{\mathtt{size}}(\varphi)\geq n_{\varphi}
, the formula
\textit{\small{Garb}}(\varphi)
is well-defined.
The formulae
\textit{\small{Alloc}}(\psi)
and
\textit{\small{Garb}}(\psi)
are defined accordingly.
Obviously,
Alloc
is equal to

\textit{\small{Alloc}}(\varphi)\ast\textit{\small{Alloc}}(\psi)
modulo associativity and commutativity
of the separating conjunction
\ast
.
Hence, by taking advantage of the axioms
(

A
∗

7

)
and
(

A
∗

9

)
, we have

\vdash_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathsf{C}}(\ast)}\textit{\small{Alloc}}\Leftrightarrow\textit{\small{Alloc}}(\varphi)\ast\textit{\small{Alloc}}(\psi).

Let us now look at
\textit{\small{Garb}}(\varphi)
and
\textit{\small{Garb}}(\psi)
.
We aim at deriving

\vdash_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathsf{C}}(\ast)}\textit{\small{Garb}}\Rightarrow\textit{\small{Garb}}(\varphi)\ast\textit{\small{Garb}}(\psi).

Since
\varphi
is a core type, we know that if
\max_{\mathtt{size}}(\varphi)<\alpha
then,
by definition of
\max_{\mathtt{size}}(\varphi)
,

\lnot\mathtt{size}\geq\max_{\mathtt{size}}(\varphi)+1\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\varphi
.
A similar analysis can be done for
\psi
, which leads to the two following equivalences, by definition of
\textit{\small{Garb}}(\varphi)
and
\textit{\small{Garb}}(\psi)
:

•

\lnot\mathtt{size}\geq\max_{\mathtt{size}}(\varphi)+1\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\varphi
if and only if

\textit{\small{Garb}}(\varphi)=(\mathtt{size}=\max_{\mathtt{size}}(\varphi){-}n_{\varphi})
,

•

\lnot\mathtt{size}\geq\max_{\mathtt{size}}(\psi)+1\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\psi
if and only if

\textit{\small{Garb}}(\psi)=(\mathtt{size}=\max_{\mathtt{size}}(\psi){-}n_{\psi})
.

By definition of
Garb
,
B.

and
C.
,
we know that
\textit{\small{Garb}}=(\mathtt{size}=\max_{\mathtt{size}}({\langle\mbox{$\ast$}\rangle}(\varphi,\psi))\mathbin{\text{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raise 4.30554pt\hbox{.}\cr$-$\cr}}}}n)

holds if and only if

\lnot\mathtt{size}\geq\max_{\mathtt{size}}(\varphi)+1\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\varphi
and

{\lnot\mathtt{size}\geq\max_{\mathtt{size}}(\varphi)+1\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\psi}
.
From
n\leq\max_{\mathtt{size}}({\langle\mbox{$\ast$}\rangle}(\varphi,\psi))
and
by relying on the previous two equivalences, this allows us to conclude that:

D
.

\textit{\small{Garb}}(\varphi)=(\mathtt{size}=\max_{\mathtt{size}}(\varphi){-}n_{\varphi})
and
\textit{\small{Garb}}(\psi)=(\mathtt{size}=\max_{\mathtt{size}}(\psi){-}n_{\psi})

if and only if

\textit{\small{Garb}}=(\mathtt{size}=\max_{\mathtt{size}}({\langle\mbox{$\ast$}\rangle}(\varphi,\psi))-n)
.

To show
\vdash_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathsf{C}}(\ast)}\textit{\small{Garb}}\Rightarrow(\textit{\small{Garb}}(\varphi)\ast\textit{\small{Garb}}(\psi))
,
we split the proof depending on whether

\textit{\small{Garb}}(\varphi)=(\mathtt{size}=\max_{\mathtt{size}}(\varphi){-}n_{\varphi})

and
\textit{\small{Garb}}(\psi)=(\mathtt{size}=\max_{\mathtt{size}}(\psi){-}n_{\psi})
hold.

case:
\textit{\small{Garb}}(\varphi)\neq(\mathtt{size}=\max_{\mathtt{size}}(\varphi){-}n_{\varphi})

and
\textit{\small{Garb}}(\psi)\neq(\mathtt{size}=\max_{\mathtt{size}}(\psi){-}n_{\psi})
:

We have
\textit{\small{Garb}}(\varphi)=(\mathtt{size}{\geq}\max_{\mathtt{size}}(\varphi){-}n_{\varphi})

and
\textit{\small{Garb}}(\psi)=(\mathtt{size}{\geq}\max_{\mathtt{size}}(\psi){-}n_{\psi})
.
By definition of
Garb
and
D.
,

\textit{\small{Garb}}=(\mathtt{size}\geq\max_{\mathtt{size}}({\langle\mbox{$\ast$}\rangle}(\varphi,\psi))-n)
.
By
n=n_{\varphi}+n_{\psi}
and
A.
,

\max_{\mathtt{size}}({\langle\mbox{$\ast$}\rangle}(\varphi,\psi))-n=(\max_{\mathtt{size}}(\varphi){-}n_{\varphi})+(\max_{\mathtt{size}}(\psi){-}n_{\psi})
.
By definition of the core formula
\mathtt{size}\geq\beta
,

Garb
is already equivalent to
\textit{\small{Garb}}(\varphi)\ast\textit{\small{Garb}}(\psi)
,
modulo associativity and commutativity
of the separating conjunction
\ast
.
Hence, by taking advantage of the axioms
(

A
∗

7

)
and
(

A
∗

9

)
,
we have

\vdash_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathsf{C}}(\ast)}\textit{\small{Garb}}\Rightarrow\textit{\small{Garb}}(\varphi)\ast\textit{\small{Garb}}(\psi).

case:
\textit{\small{Garb}}(\varphi)=(\mathtt{size}=\max_{\mathtt{size}}(\varphi){-}n_{\varphi})

and
\textit{\small{Garb}}(\psi)\neq(\mathtt{size}=\max_{\mathtt{size}}(\psi){-}n_{\psi})
:

We have
\textit{\small{Garb}}(\psi)=(\mathtt{size}{\geq}\max_{\mathtt{size}}(\psi){-}n_{\psi})
and,
by definition of
Garb
and
D.
,
along with
n=n_{\varphi}+n_{\psi}
and
A.
,

\textit{\small{Garb}}=(\mathtt{size}\geq(\max_{\mathtt{size}}(\varphi){-}n_{\varphi})+(\max_{\mathtt{size}}(\psi){-}n_{\psi}))
.
In this case,

\textit{\small{Garb}}\Rightarrow\textit{\small{Garb}}(\varphi)\ast\textit{\small{Garb}}(\psi)

is an instantiation of the following valid formula with
\beta_{1}=\max_{\mathtt{size}}(\varphi){-}n_{\varphi}
and
\beta_{2}=\max_{\mathtt{size}}(\psi)-n_{\psi}
:

\mathtt{size}\geq\beta_{1}+\beta_{2}\Rightarrow\mathtt{size}=\beta_{1}\ast\mathtt{size}\geq\beta_{2}.

The derivability of this formula in
\mathcal{H}_{\mathsf{C}}(\ast)
is proven by induction on
\beta_{1}

(see Appendix
B
).

case:
\textit{\small{Garb}}(\varphi)\neq(\mathtt{size}=\max_{\mathtt{size}}(\varphi){-}n_{\varphi})

and
\textit{\small{Garb}}(\psi)=(\mathtt{size}=\max_{\mathtt{size}}(\psi){-}n_{\psi})
:

Analogously to the previous case, we have
\textit{\small{Garb}}(\varphi)=(\mathtt{size}{\geq}\max_{\mathtt{size}}(\varphi){-}n_{\varphi})

and

\textit{\small{Garb}}=(\mathtt{size}\geq(\max_{\mathtt{size}}(\varphi){-}n_{\varphi})+(\max_{\mathtt{size}}(\psi){-}n_{\psi}))
.
We instantiate the theorem

\mathtt{size}\geq\beta_{1}+\beta_{2}\Rightarrow\mathtt{size}=\beta_{1}\ast\mathtt{size}\geq\beta_{2},

shown derivable in the previous case of the proof,
with
\beta_{1}=\max_{\mathtt{size}}(\psi){-}n_{\psi}
and
\beta_{2}=\max_{\mathtt{size}}(\varphi)-n_{\varphi}
.
This corresponds to
\textit{\small{Garb}}\Rightarrow\textit{\small{Garb}}(\psi)\ast\textit{\small{Garb}}(\varphi)
.
Afterwards, by commutativity of the separating conjunction (axiom
(

A
∗

7

)
) and propositional reasoning, we conclude that
\vdash_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathsf{C}}(\ast)}\textit{\small{Garb}}\Rightarrow\textit{\small{Garb}}(\varphi)\ast\textit{\small{Garb}}(\psi)
.

case:
\textit{\small{Garb}}(\varphi)=(\mathtt{size}=\max_{\mathtt{size}}(\varphi){-}n_{\varphi})

and
\textit{\small{Garb}}(\psi)=(\mathtt{size}=\max_{\mathtt{size}}(\psi){-}n_{\psi})
:

By
D.
,
n=n_{\varphi}+n_{\psi}
and
A.
,

\textit{\small{Garb}}=(\mathtt{size}=(\max_{\mathtt{size}}(\varphi){-}n_{\varphi})+(\max_{\mathtt{size}}(\psi){-}n_{\psi}))
.
In this case,

\textit{\small{Garb}}\Rightarrow\textit{\small{Garb}}(\varphi)\ast\textit{\small{Garb}}(\psi)

is an instantiation of the following valid formula, with
\beta_{1}=\max_{\mathtt{size}}(\varphi){-}n_{\varphi}
and
\beta_{2}=\max_{\mathtt{size}}(\psi)-n_{\psi}
:

\mathtt{size}=\beta_{1}+\beta_{2}\Rightarrow\mathtt{size}=\beta_{1}\ast\mathtt{size}=\beta_{2}.

Its derivation in
\mathcal{H}_{\mathsf{C}}(\ast)
can be found in Appendix
B
.

Thanks to the case analysis above, we conclude that
\vdash_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathsf{C}}(\ast)}\textit{\small{Garb}}\Rightarrow\textit{\small{Garb}}(\varphi)\ast\textit{\small{Garb}}(\psi)
.
Thus,
\vdash_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathsf{C}}(\ast)}\textit{\small{Alloc}}\ast\textit{\small{Garb}}\Rightarrow(\textit{\small{Alloc}}(\varphi)\ast\textit{\small{Garb}}(\varphi))\ast(\textit{\small{Alloc}}(\psi)\ast\textit{\small{Garb}}(\psi))
.
Indeed,

1

Previously derived

To conclude this step of the proof,
it is sufficient to show
{\vdash_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathsf{C}}(\ast)}\textit{\small{Alloc}}(\varphi)\ast\textit{\small{Garb}}(\varphi)\Rightarrow\varphi^{(1)}}

\vdash_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathsf{C}}(\ast)}\textit{\small{Alloc}}(\psi)\ast\textit{\small{Garb}}(\psi)\Rightarrow\psi^{(1)}
.
Indeed, by relying on the rule
3
,
we then obtain
\vdash_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathsf{C}}(\ast)}\textit{\small{Alloc}}\ast\textit{\small{Garb}}\Rightarrow\varphi^{(1)}\ast\psi^{(1)}

Below, we show
\vdash_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathsf{C}}(\ast)}\textit{\small{Alloc}}(\varphi)\ast\textit{\small{Garb}}(\varphi)\Rightarrow\varphi^{(1)}
.
The developments of

We recall that the formula
\textit{\small{Alloc}}(\varphi)
is defined as

\textit{\small{Alloc}}(\varphi)\,=\,\Asterisk\{\mathtt{alloc}(\mathtt{x}_{i})\wedge\mathtt{size}=1\mid\mathtt{alloc}(\mathtt{x}_{i})\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\varphi\}.

First of all, let us show that
\vdash_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathsf{C}}(\ast)}\textit{\small{Alloc}}(\varphi)\ast\top\Rightarrow\bigwedge\{\mathtt{alloc}(\mathtt{x}_{i})\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\varphi\mid i\in[1,n]\}
.
The proof is divided in three cases:

case:
\{\mathtt{alloc}(\mathtt{x}_{i})\wedge\mathtt{size}=1\mid\mathtt{alloc}(\mathtt{x}_{i})\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\varphi\}=\emptyset
:

In this case, the formula
we want to derive is syntactically equal to

\top\ast\top\Rightarrow\top
,
which is derivable by propositional reasoning.

case:
{\rm card}(\{\mathtt{alloc}(\mathtt{x}_{i})\wedge\mathtt{size}=1\mid\mathtt{alloc}(\mathtt{x}_{i})\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\varphi\})=1
:

In this case,
the formula we want to derive is syntactically equal to

(\mathtt{alloc}(\mathtt{x})\land\mathtt{size}=1)\ast\top\Rightarrow\mathtt{alloc}(\mathtt{x})
.
Therefore, it is derivable in
\mathcal{H}_{\mathsf{C}}(\ast)
by
(

I
∗

17

)

and

∗

-Intro:
.

{\rm card}(\{\mathtt{alloc}(\mathtt{x}_{i})\wedge\mathtt{size}=1\mid\mathtt{alloc}(\mathtt{x}_{i})\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\varphi\}){\geq}2
:

In the derivation below, we write
\textit{\small{Alloc}}(\varphi)^{-i}
for

\Asterisk\{\mathtt{alloc}(\mathtt{x}_{j})\land\mathtt{size}=1\mid j\in[1,n]\setminus\{i\},\mathtt{alloc}(\mathtt{x}_{j})\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\varphi\}
.
Roughly speaking,
\textit{\small{Alloc}}(\varphi)^{-i}

.

,

\textit{\small{Alloc}}(\varphi)^{-i}

\top
.
We have

1

\textit{\small{Alloc}}(\varphi)\ast\top\Rightarrow

\hskip 9.24994pt(\mathtt{alloc}(\mathtt{x}_{i})\land\mathtt{size}=1)\ast(\textit{\small{Alloc}}(\varphi)^{-i}\ast\top)

\textit{\small{Alloc}}(\varphi)^{-i}\ast\top\Rightarrow\top

Step 3, add the missing literals

From the first and second step of the proof, and by propositional reasoning,

\vdash_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathsf{C}}(\ast)}{\langle\mbox{$\ast$}\rangle}(\varphi,\psi)\Rightarrow\varphi^{(1)}\ast\psi^{(1)}
.
We now rely on
{\langle\mbox{$\ast$}\rangle}(\varphi,\psi)
to add to
\varphi^{(1)}
and
\psi^{(1)}
missing literals from
\varphi
and
\psi
, respectively.
We add the literals progressively,
building a sequence of formulae
\varphi^{(1)}\!\ast\psi^{(1)}
,

\varphi^{(2)}\!\ast\psi^{(2)}
,
\dots
,
\varphi^{(k)}\!\ast\psi^{(k)}
, where for all
i\in[1,k]
,

\varphi^{(i)}
and
\psi^{(i)}
are conjunctions of core formulae such that

\vdash_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathsf{C}}(\ast)}{\langle\mbox{$\ast$}\rangle}(\varphi,\psi)\Rightarrow\varphi^{(i)}\ast\psi^{(i)}
, and for all
j\in[1,i]
,
\varphi^{(j)}\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\varphi^{(i)}
and
\psi^{(j)}\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\psi^{(i)}
.
Fundamentally, we obtain
\varphi=\varphi^{(k)}

and
\psi=\psi^{(k)}

(modulo associativity and commutativity of the classical conjunction),
which allows us to derive
\vdash_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathsf{C}}(\ast)}{\langle\mbox{$\ast$}\rangle}(\varphi,\psi)\Rightarrow\varphi\ast\psi
, ending the proof.
Below, we focus on the formula
\varphi^{(i)}
and
\varphi
.
Since
{\langle\mbox{$\ast$}\rangle}(\varphi,\psi)
is equal to
{\langle\mbox{$\ast$}\rangle}(\psi,\varphi)

(by a quick inspection of the definition)
and the separating conjunction is commutative (axiom
(

A
∗

7

)
), a similar analysis can be done for
\psi^{(i)}
and
\psi
.
Thus, we assume that
\vdash_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathsf{C}}(\ast)}{\langle\mbox{$\ast$}\rangle}(\varphi,\psi)\Rightarrow\varphi^{(i)}\ast\psi^{(i)}
holds,
where in particular
\varphi^{(1)}\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\varphi^{(i)}
and
\psi^{(1)}\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\psi^{(i)}
,
and that there is a literal

that does not appear in
\varphi^{(i)}
.

,

Corollary 10

(Star elimination)
.

Let
\mathtt{X}\subseteq_{\mathsf{fin}}\mathsf{VAR}
and
\alpha\geq{\rm card}(\mathtt{X})
.
Let
\varphi
and
\psi

in
\mathsf{CoreTypes}(\mathtt{X},\alpha)
.
There is

\chi
in
\mathsf{Conj}(\mathsf{Core}(\mathtt{X},2\alpha))
such that

\vdash_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathsf{C}}(\ast)}\varphi\ast\psi\Leftrightarrow\chi
.

Proof 5.10
.

If both
\varphi
and
\psi
are satisfiable, the results holds directly
by Lemma
9
,
as
{\langle\mbox{$\ast$}\rangle}(\varphi,\psi)
is
in
\mathsf{Conj}(\mathsf{Core}(\mathtt{X},\alpha+\alpha))
.
Otherwise,
let us treat the case where one of the two formulas is unsatisfiable.
For instance, assume that
\varphi
is unsatisfiable.
Then
\vdash_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathsf{C}}}\varphi\Rightarrow\bot
by completeness of
\mathcal{H}_{\mathsf{C}}

(Lemma
3
) and,
\mathcal{H}_{\mathsf{C}}(\ast)
includes
\mathcal{H}_{\mathsf{C}}
,
\vdash_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathsf{C}}(\ast)}\varphi\Rightarrow\bot
.
By
the rule

∗

-Intro:
and by the axiom
(

I
∗

13

)
, we get
\vdash_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathsf{C}}(\ast)}\varphi*\psi\Rightarrow\bot
.
Thus
\chi
can take the value
\lnot(\mathtt{x}=\mathtt{x})
.
The case where
\psi
is not satisfiable is analogous, thanks to
(

A
∗

7

)
.

By the distributivity axiom
(

I
∗

11

)
, Corollary
10

is extended from core types to arbitrary Boolean combinations of core formulae.

\mathcal{H}_{\mathsf{C}}(\ast)
is therefore complete for
\mathsf{SL(}\ast,\mathtt{alloc}\mathsf{)}
.
In order to derive a valid formula
\varphi\in\mathsf{SL(}\ast,\mathtt{alloc}\mathsf{)}
, we repeatedly apply
the elimination of
\ast
in a bottom-up fashion, starting from the leaves of
\varphi
(which
are Boolean combinations of core formulae) and obtaining a Boolean combination of core formulae
\psi
that is equivalent to
\varphi
.
Then, we rely on the completeness of
\mathcal{H}_{\mathsf{C}}
(Theorem
5
) to prove that
\psi
is derivable.

Theorem 11
.

A formula
\varphi
in
\mathsf{SL(}\ast,\mathtt{alloc}\mathsf{)}
is valid iff
\vdash_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathsf{C}}(\ast)}\varphi
.

Proof 5.11
.

Soundness of the proof system
\mathcal{H}_{\mathsf{C}}(\ast)
has been already established earlier.

As far as the completeness proof is concerned, we need to show that for every formula
\varphi

in
\mathsf{SL(}\ast,\mathtt{alloc}\mathsf{)}
, there is a Boolean combination of core formulae
\psi
such that

\vdash_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathsf{C}}(\ast)}\varphi\Leftrightarrow\psi
. In order to conclude the proof,
when
\varphi
is valid for
\mathsf{SL(}\ast,\mathtt{alloc}\mathsf{)}
, by soundness of
\mathcal{H}_{\mathsf{C}}(\ast)
, we obtain that

\psi
is valid too and therefore
\vdash_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathsf{C}}(\ast)}\psi

as
\mathcal{H}_{\mathsf{C}}
is a subsystem of
\mathcal{H}_{\mathsf{C}}(\ast)
and
\mathcal{H}_{\mathsf{C}}
is complete by
Theorem
5
. By propositional reasoning, we get that

\vdash_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathsf{C}}(\ast)}\varphi
.

To show that every formula
\varphi
has a provably equivalent Boolean combination
of core formulae, we heavily rely on Corollary
10
. The proof is by simple induction
on the number of occurrences of
\ast
in
\varphi
that are not involved in the definition of some core
formula of the form
\mathtt{size}\geq\beta
. For the base case, when
\varphi

has no occurrence of the separating conjunction,
\mathtt{x}=\mathtt{y}
and
\mathtt{x}\hookrightarrow\mathtt{y}
are already core formulae, and
\mathtt{emp}
is logically equivalent to
\neg\mathtt{size}\geq 1
.

Before performing the induction step, let us observe that in
\mathcal{H}_{\mathsf{C}}(\ast)
, the replacement of provably equivalent
formulae holds true, which is stated as follows:

R0

Let
\varphi,\varphi^{\prime}
and
\psi
be
formulae of
\mathsf{SL(}\ast,\mathtt{alloc}\mathsf{)}
such that

\vdash_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathsf{C}}(\ast)}\varphi\Leftrightarrow\varphi^{\prime}
. Then,

\vdash_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathsf{C}}(\ast)}\psi[\varphi]_{\rho}\Rightarrow\psi[\varphi^{\prime}]_{\rho}

Above,
\psi[\varphi]_{\rho}
refers to the formula
\psi
in which the subformula
at the occurrence
\rho
(in the standard sense) is replaced by
\varphi
.
(
\varphi
and
\varphi^{\prime}
are therefore placed at the same occurrence.)

To prove
R0
, we first note that the following rules can be shown admissible in
\mathcal{H}_{\mathsf{C}}(\ast)
:

\text{}\frac{\displaystyle\varphi\Leftrightarrow\varphi^{\prime}}{\displaystyle\neg\varphi\Leftrightarrow\neg\varphi^{\prime}}\ \ \ \text{}\frac{\displaystyle\varphi\Leftrightarrow\varphi^{\prime}}{\displaystyle\varphi\vee\psi\Leftrightarrow\varphi^{\prime}\vee\psi}\ \ \ \text{}\frac{\displaystyle\varphi\Leftrightarrow\varphi^{\prime}}{\displaystyle\varphi\wedge\psi\Leftrightarrow\varphi^{\prime}\wedge\psi}

Admissibility of such rules is a direct consequence of the presence of axioms and modus ponens for the propositional calculus.
As a consequence of the presence of the rule

∗

-Intro:
in
\mathcal{H}_{\mathsf{C}}(\ast)
, the rule below is also admissible:

\text{}\frac{\displaystyle\varphi\Leftrightarrow\varphi^{\prime}}{\displaystyle\varphi\ast\psi\Leftrightarrow\varphi^{\prime}\ast\psi}

Consequently, by structural induction on
\psi
, one can conclude that

\vdash_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathsf{C}}(\ast)}\varphi\Leftrightarrow\varphi^{\prime}

implies
\vdash_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathsf{C}}(\ast)}\psi[\varphi]_{\rho}\Rightarrow\psi[\varphi^{\prime}]_{\rho}

(the axiom
(

A
∗

7

)
needs to be used here).

Assume that
\varphi
is a formula in
\mathsf{SL(}\ast,\mathtt{alloc}\mathsf{)}
with
n+1
occurrences of the separating conjunction not involved in the definition of some

\mathtt{size}\geq\beta
(
n\geq 0
).
Let
\psi
be a subformula of
\varphi
(at the occurrence
\rho
) of the form
\psi_{1}\ast\psi_{2}
such that

\psi_{1}
and
\psi_{2}
are Boolean combinations of
core formulae, in
\mathsf{Bool}(\mathsf{Core}(\mathtt{X},\alpha_{1}))
and
\mathsf{Bool}(\mathsf{Core}(\mathtt{X},\alpha_{2}))
.
By pure propositional reasoning, one can show that there are formulae in disjunctive normal form

\psi_{1}^{1}\vee\cdots\vee\psi_{1}^{n_{1}}
and
{\psi_{2}^{1}\vee\cdots\vee\psi_{2}^{n_{2}}}

such that
\vdash_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathsf{C}}}\psi_{i}\Leftrightarrow\psi_{i}^{1}\vee\cdots\vee\psi_{i}^{n_{i}}

for
i\in\{1,2\}
and moreover, all the
\psi_{i}^{j}
’s are core types in
\mathsf{CoreTypes}(\mathtt{X},\max({\rm card}(\mathtt{X}),\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}))
.
Again, by using propositional reasoning but this time using also the axiom
(

I
∗

11

)
for distributivity, we have

\vdash_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathsf{C}}(\ast)}\psi_{1}\ast\psi_{2}\Leftrightarrow\bigvee_{j_{1}\in[1,n_{1}],j_{2}\in[1,n_{2}]}\psi_{1}^{j_{1}}\ast\psi_{2}^{j_{2}}.

We now rely on Corollary
10
and derive that there is a
conjunction of core formulae
\psi^{j_{1},j_{2}}
in
\mathsf{Conj}(\mathsf{Core}(\mathtt{X},2\max({\rm card}(\mathtt{X}),\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2})))
such that

\vdash_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathsf{C}}(\ast)}\psi_{1}^{j_{1}}\ast\psi_{2}^{j_{2}}\Leftrightarrow\psi^{j_{1},j_{2}}
.
By propositional reasoning, we get

\vdash_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathsf{C}}(\ast)}\psi_{1}\ast\psi_{2}\Leftrightarrow\bigvee_{j_{1}\in[1,n_{1}],j_{2}\in[1,n_{2}]}\psi^{j_{1},j_{2}}.

Consequently (thanks to the property
R0
), we obtain

\vdash_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathsf{C}}(\ast)}\varphi\Leftrightarrow\varphi[\bigvee_{j_{1}\in[1,n_{1}],j_{2}\in[1,n_{2}]}\psi^{j_{1},j_{2}}]_{\rho}

Note that the right-hand side formula has
n
occurrences of the separating
conjunction that are not involved in the definition of some core
formula of the form
\mathtt{size}\geq\beta
. The induction hypothesis applies, which concludes the proof.

6.
A constructive elimination of
\mathbin{-\mkern-6.0mu*}
leading to full completeness

(
\mathbf{A^{\mathbin{-\mkern-6.0mu*}}_{21}}
)

(\mathtt{size}=1\land\bigwedge_{\mathtt{x}\in\mathtt{X}}\lnot\mathtt{alloc}(\mathtt{x}))\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}\top\!\ \blacktriangleleft\!\!\!\left[\mathtt{X}\subseteq_{\mathsf{fin}}\mathsf{VAR}\right]

\ast
-Adj
:

\text{}\frac{\displaystyle\varphi\ast\psi\Rightarrow\chi}{\displaystyle\varphi\Rightarrow(\psi\mathbin{-\mkern-6.0mu*}\chi)}{}

\mathbin{-\mkern-6.0mu*}
-Adj
:

\text{}\frac{\displaystyle\varphi\Rightarrow(\psi\mathbin{-\mkern-6.0mu*}\chi)}{\displaystyle\varphi\ast\psi\Rightarrow\chi}{}

Figure 7.
Additional axioms and rules for handling the separating implication.

In order to obtain the final proof system
\mathcal{H}_{\mathsf{C}}(\ast,\mathbin{-\mkern-6.0mu*})
, we add the axioms and rules
from Figure
7
to the proof system
\mathcal{H}_{\mathsf{C}}(\ast)
. These new axioms
and rules are dedicated to the separating implication.
The axioms involving
\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}
(kind of dual of
\mathbin{-\mkern-6.0mu*}
, introduced in Section
2
) express that it is always
possible to extend a given heap with an extra cell, and that the address and the content of this cell can be fixed arbitrarily
(provided it is not already allocated). The adjunction rules
7
and
7
are from the Hilbert-style axiomatisation
of Boolean
BI
[
GLW06
, Section 2]
.
One can observe that, in
\mathcal{H}_{\mathsf{C}}(\ast,\mathbin{-\mkern-6.0mu*})
,
the axioms
(

∗

)
,
(

∗

)
and
(

∗

)

of
\mathcal{H}_{\mathsf{C}}(\ast)

are derivable.

Lemma 12
.

The axioms
(

I
∗

)
,
(

∗

)
and
(

∗

)

are derivable
in

The derivations of

Fundamentally,
\mathcal{H}_{\mathsf{C}}(\ast,\mathbin{-\mkern-6.0mu*})
enjoys the
\mathbin{-\mkern-6.0mu*}
elimination property, as shown
below. Actually, we state the property with the help of
\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}
as we find the related statements and developments more intuitive.

Lemma 13
.

Let
\mathtt{X}\subseteq_{\mathsf{fin}}\mathsf{VAR}
and
\alpha\geq{\rm card}(\mathtt{X})
.
Let
\varphi
and
\psi
in
\mathsf{CoreTypes}(\mathtt{X},\alpha)
. There is a conjunction

\chi\in\mathsf{Conj}(\mathsf{Core}(\mathtt{X},\alpha))

such that
\vdash_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathsf{C}}(\ast,\mathbin{-\mkern-6.0mu*})}(\varphi\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}\psi)\Leftrightarrow\chi
.

Proof 6.1

(Structure of the proof of Lemma
13
)
.

In the proof of Lemma
13
, the formula
\chi

is explicitly constructed from
\varphi
and
\psi
, following a pattern analogous
to the construction of
{\langle\mbox{$\ast$}\rangle}(.\,,.)
in Figure
6
(see forthcoming
Figure
8
).
The derivation of the equivalence
(\varphi\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}\psi)\Leftrightarrow\chi

is shown as follows. First, the formulae

\chi\ast\varphi\Rightarrow\psi
and

\neg\chi\ast\varphi\Rightarrow\neg\psi
are shown valid
(by using semantical means). As
\mathcal{H}_{\mathsf{C}}(\ast)
is complete for
\mathsf{SL(}\ast,\mathtt{alloc}\mathsf{)}
, it is a subsystem of

\mathcal{H}_{\mathsf{C}}(\ast,\mathbin{-\mkern-6.0mu*})
, and the formulae
\varphi
,
\psi
and
\chi
are Boolean combinations
of core formulae, we get
\vdash_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathsf{C}}(\ast,\mathbin{-\mkern-6.0mu*})}\chi\ast\varphi\Rightarrow\psi

and
\vdash_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathsf{C}}(\ast,\mathbin{-\mkern-6.0mu*})}\neg\chi\ast\varphi\Rightarrow\neg\psi
.
The latter theorem leads
to
\vdash_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathsf{C}}(\ast,\mathbin{-\mkern-6.0mu*})}(\varphi\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}\psi)\Rightarrow\chi

by using the definition of
\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}
and the rule
7
.
For the other direction, in order to show that
\vdash_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathsf{C}}(\ast,\mathbin{-\mkern-6.0mu*})}\chi\Rightarrow(\varphi\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}\psi)

holds, we take advantage of the admissibility of the theorem
(

I

-
⁣
∗

⁢

.17

)

(see Lemma
14
below)
for which an instance is

(\varphi\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}\top)\wedge(\varphi\mathbin{-\mkern-6.0mu*}\psi)\Rightarrow(\varphi\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}(\top\wedge\psi))
.
From
\vdash_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathsf{C}}(\ast,\mathbin{-\mkern-6.0mu*})}\chi\ast\varphi\Rightarrow\psi

and by
7
we have

\vdash_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathsf{C}}(\ast,\mathbin{-\mkern-6.0mu*})}\chi\Rightarrow(\varphi\mathbin{-\mkern-6.0mu*}\psi)
.
Therefore, the main technical development lies in the proof of

\vdash_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathsf{C}}(\ast,\mathbin{-\mkern-6.0mu*})}\chi\Rightarrow(\varphi\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}\top)
,
which allows us to take advantage of
(

I

-
⁣
∗

⁢

.17

)
,
and leads to
\vdash_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathsf{C}}(\ast,\mathbin{-\mkern-6.0mu*})}\chi\Rightarrow(\varphi\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}\psi)
by propositional reasoning.

In order to formalise the proof of Lemma
13
sketched above, we start by establishing
several admissible axioms and rules (Lemma
14
).
Afterwards, we define the formula
\chi
and show
the validity of
\chi\ast\varphi\Rightarrow\psi
and

\neg\chi\ast\varphi\Rightarrow\neg\psi
(Lemma
15
).
Then, come the final bits of the proof of Lemma

(see page
6.4
).

Lemma 14
.

The following rules and axioms are admissible in
\mathcal{H}_{\mathsf{C}}(\ast,\mathbin{-\mkern-6.0mu*})
:

(
\mathbf{I^{\mathbin{-\mkern-6.0mu*}}_{\ref{lemma:septractionadmissible}.1}}
)

\bot\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}\varphi\Rightarrow\bot

(
\mathbf{I^{\mathbin{-\mkern-6.0mu*}}_{\ref{lemma:septractionadmissible}.3}}
)

\varphi\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}\bot\Rightarrow\bot

(
\mathbf{I^{\mathbin{-\mkern-6.0mu*}}_{\ref{lemma:septractionadmissible}.5}}
)

\varphi\ast(\varphi\mathbin{-\mkern-6.0mu*}\psi)\Rightarrow\psi

(
\mathbf{I^{\mathbin{-\mkern-6.0mu*}}_{\ref{lemma:septractionadmissible}.7}}
)

\text{}\frac{\displaystyle\varphi\Rightarrow\psi}{\displaystyle\varphi\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}\chi\Rightarrow\psi\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}\chi}{}

(
\mathbf{I^{\mathbin{-\mkern-6.0mu*}}_{\ref{lemma:septractionadmissible}.9}}
)

\text{}\frac{\displaystyle\varphi\Rightarrow\psi}{\displaystyle\chi\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}\varphi\Rightarrow\chi\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}\psi}{}

(
\mathbf{I^{\mathbin{-\mkern-6.0mu*}}_{\ref{lemma:septractionadmissible}.11}}
)

\varphi\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}(\psi\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}\chi)\ \Leftrightarrow\ (\varphi*\psi)\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}\chi

(
\mathbf{I^{\mathbin{-\mkern-6.0mu*}}_{\ref{lemma:septractionadmissible}.13}}
)

(\varphi\vee\psi)\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}\chi\ \Leftrightarrow\ (\varphi\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}\chi)\vee(\psi\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}\chi)

(
\mathbf{I^{\mathbin{-\mkern-6.0mu*}}_{\ref{lemma:septractionadmissible}.15}}
)

\chi\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}(\varphi\vee\psi)\ \Leftrightarrow\ (\chi\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}\varphi)\vee(\chi\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}\psi)

(
\mathbf{I^{\mathbin{-\mkern-6.0mu*}}_{\ref{lemma:septractionadmissible}.17}}
)

(\varphi\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}\psi)\wedge(\varphi\mathbin{-\mkern-6.0mu*}\chi)\Rightarrow(\varphi\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}\psi\wedge\chi)

(
\mathbf{I^{\mathbin{-\mkern-6.0mu*}}_{\ref{lemma:septractionadmissible}.19}}
)

\mathtt{x}=\mathtt{y}\land(\varphi\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}\psi)\Rightarrow(\varphi\land\mathtt{x}=\mathtt{y}\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}\psi)

(
\mathbf{I^{\mathbin{-\mkern-6.0mu*}}_{\ref{lemma:septractionadmissible}.21}}
)

\mathtt{x}\neq\mathtt{y}\land(\varphi\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}\psi)\Rightarrow(\varphi\land\mathtt{x}\neq\mathtt{y}\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}\psi)

(
\mathbf{I^{\mathbin{-\mkern-6.0mu*}}_{\ref{lemma:septractionadmissible}.23}}
)

(\varphi_{\mathtt{size}}\land\bigwedge_{\mathtt{x}\in\mathtt{X}}\lnot\mathtt{alloc}(\mathtt{x}))\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}\top
,

(

The proof of Lemma

can be found in Appendix

.

Let
\varphi
and
\psi

in
\mathsf{Conj}(\mathsf{Core}(\mathtt{X},\alpha))
.

,

Lemma 15
.

Let
\mathtt{X}\subseteq_{\mathsf{fin}}\mathsf{VAR}
,
\alpha\geq{\rm card}(\mathtt{X})
and

\varphi
,
\psi
be satisfiable core types in
\mathsf{CoreTypes}(\mathtt{X},\alpha)
.
The formulae
{\langle\mbox{$\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}$}\rangle}(\varphi,\psi)\ast\varphi\Rightarrow\psi
and

(\lnot{\langle\mbox{$\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}$}\rangle}(\varphi,\psi))\ast\varphi\Rightarrow\lnot\psi
are valid.

Before presenting the proof for Lemma
15
,
let us observe that since we aim at proving the derivability of

\varphi\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}\psi\Leftrightarrow{\langle\mbox{$\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}$}\rangle}(\varphi,\psi)
in
\mathcal{H}_{\mathsf{C}}(\ast,\mathbin{-\mkern-6.0mu*})
,
the validity of
{(\lnot{\langle\mbox{$\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}$}\rangle}(\varphi,\psi))\ast\varphi\Rightarrow\lnot\psi}
should not surprise the reader.
Indeed, by replacing
{\langle\mbox{$\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}$}\rangle}(\varphi,\psi)
with
\varphi\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}\psi
we obtain
(\lnot(\varphi\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}\psi))\ast\varphi\Rightarrow\lnot\psi
which, unfolding the definition of
\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}
, is equivalent to the valid formula
(\varphi\mathbin{-\mkern-6.0mu*}\lnot\psi)\ast\varphi\Rightarrow\lnot\psi
(see
(

I

-
⁣
∗

⁢

.5

)
in Lemma
14
).
On the other hand, the fact that
{\langle\mbox{$\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}$}\rangle}(\varphi,\psi)\ast\varphi\Rightarrow\psi
is valid can be puzzling at first, as the formula
(\varphi\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}\psi)\ast\varphi\Rightarrow\psi
is not valid (in general). In its essence, Lemma
15
shows that
(\varphi\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}\psi)\ast\varphi\Rightarrow\psi
is valid whenever
\varphi
and
\psi
are restricted to core types.

\begin{array}[]{rll}&\bigwedge{\scalebox{0.8}{$\left\{\mathtt{x}\sim\mathtt{y}\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\{\varphi\mathbin{|}\psi\}\,\middle|\,{{{\begin{array}[]{r}\sim\in\{=,\neq\}\end{array}}}}\right\}$}}&\land\bigwedge{\scalebox{0.8}{$\left\{\mathtt{alloc}(\mathtt{x})\,\middle|\,{{{\begin{array}[]{r}\lnot\mathtt{alloc}(\mathtt{x})\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\varphi\\
\mathtt{alloc}(\mathtt{x})\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\psi\end{array}}}}\right\}$}}\\
\land&\bigwedge{\scalebox{0.8}{$\left\{\lnot\mathtt{alloc}(\mathtt{x})\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\psi\right\}$}}&\land\bigwedge{\scalebox{0.8}{$\left\{\lnot\mathtt{alloc}(\mathtt{x})\,\middle|\,{{{\begin{array}[]{r}\mathtt{alloc}(\mathtt{x})\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\varphi\end{array}}}}\right\}$}}\\
\land&\bigwedge{\scalebox{0.8}{$\left\{\lnot\mathtt{x}{\hookrightarrow}\mathtt{y}\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\psi\right\}$}}&\land\bigwedge{\scalebox{0.8}{$\left\{\mathtt{x}\hookrightarrow\mathtt{y}\,\middle|\,{{{\begin{array}[]{r}\lnot\mathtt{alloc}(\mathtt{x})\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\varphi\\
\mathtt{x}\hookrightarrow\mathtt{y}\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\psi\end{array}}}}\right\}$}}\\
\land&\bigwedge{\scalebox{0.8}{$\left\{\mathtt{x}\neq\mathtt{x}\,\middle|\,{{{\begin{array}[]{r}\mathtt{alloc}(\mathtt{x})\land\lnot\mathtt{x}\hookrightarrow\mathtt{y}\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\varphi\\
\mathtt{x}\hookrightarrow\mathtt{y}\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\psi\end{array}}}}\right\}$}}&\land\bigwedge{\scalebox{0.8}{$\left\{\mathtt{size}\geq\beta_{2}{+}1{\mathbin{\text{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raise 6.19998pt\hbox{.}\cr$-$\cr}}}}}\beta_{1}\,\middle|\,{{{\begin{array}[]{r}\lnot\mathtt{size}\geq\beta_{1}\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\varphi\\
\mathtt{size}\geq\beta_{2}\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\psi\end{array}}}}\right\}$}}\\
\land&\bigwedge{\scalebox{0.8}{$\left\{\mathtt{x}\neq\mathtt{x}\,\middle|\,{{{\begin{array}[]{r}\mathtt{x}\hookrightarrow\mathtt{y}\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\varphi\\
\lnot\mathtt{x}\hookrightarrow\mathtt{y}\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\psi\end{array}}}}\right\}$}}&\land\bigwedge{\scalebox{0.8}{$\left\{\lnot\mathtt{size}\geq\beta_{2}{\mathbin{\text{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raise 6.19998pt\hbox{.}\cr$-$\cr}}}}}\beta_{1}\,\middle|\,{{{\begin{array}[]{r}\mathtt{size}\geq\beta_{1}\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\varphi\\
\lnot\mathtt{size}\geq\beta_{2}\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\psi\end{array}}}}\right\}$}}\\
\land&\bigwedge{\scalebox{0.8}{$\left\{\mathtt{x}\neq\mathtt{x}\,\middle|\,{{{\begin{array}[]{r}\mathtt{alloc}(\mathtt{x})\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\varphi\\
\lnot\mathtt{alloc}(\mathtt{x})\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\psi\end{array}}}}\right\}$}}\end{array}

Figure 8.
The formula
{\langle\mbox{$\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}$}\rangle}(\varphi,\psi)
.

Below, we prove that
{\langle\mbox{$\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}$}\rangle}(\varphi,\psi)\ast\varphi\Rightarrow\psi
and

(\lnot{\langle\mbox{$\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}$}\rangle}(\varphi,\psi))\ast\varphi\Rightarrow\lnot\psi
are valid, thus establishing Lemma
15
.
Notice that the proof is carried out through semantical arguments.
Since
\varphi
,
\psi
and
{\langle\mbox{$\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}$}\rangle}(\varphi,\psi)
are
conjunctions of literals built from core formulae, derivability of these two tautologies in
\mathcal{H}_{\mathsf{C}}(\ast,\mathbin{-\mkern-6.0mu*})
follows from the completeness of
\mathcal{H}_{\mathsf{C}}(\ast)
(Theorem
11
).

Proof 6.2

(Validity of
{\langle\mbox{$\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}$}\rangle}(\varphi,\psi)\ast\varphi\Rightarrow\psi
)
.

If
{\langle\mbox{$\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}$}\rangle}(\varphi,\psi)\ast\varphi
is inconsistent, then
{\langle\mbox{$\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}$}\rangle}(\varphi,\psi)\ast\varphi\Rightarrow\psi
is straightforwardly valid.
Below, we assume that
{\langle\mbox{$\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}$}\rangle}(\varphi,\psi)\ast\varphi
is
satisfiable. In particular, none of the conditions depicted in Figure
8
that result in
{\langle\mbox{$\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}$}\rangle}(\varphi,\psi)
having a literal
\mathtt{x}\neq\mathtt{x}
applies.
Let
(s,h)\models{\langle\mbox{$\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}$}\rangle}(\varphi,\psi)\ast\varphi
.
Therefore, there are two disjoint heaps
h_{1}
and
h_{2}
such that
h=h_{1}{+}h_{2}
,
(s,h_{1})\models{\langle\mbox{$\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}$}\rangle}(\varphi,\psi)

and
(s,h_{2})\models\varphi
.
We show that
(s,h)
satisfies each literal
L
in
\psi
.
We perform a simple case analysis on the shape of
L
.
Notice that, below, we have
\mathtt{x},\mathtt{y}\in\mathtt{X}
and
\beta_{2}\in[0,\alpha]
, as
\psi
is a core type in
\mathsf{CoreTypes}(\mathtt{X},\alpha)
.

case:
L\ =\ \mathtt{x}\sim\mathtt{y}
, where
\sim\in\{=,\neq\}
:

By definition of
{\langle\mbox{$\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}$}\rangle}(\varphi,\psi)
,
\mathtt{x}\sim\mathtt{y}\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}{\langle\mbox{$\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}$}\rangle}(\varphi,\psi)
and so
(s,h_{1})\models\mathtt{x}\sim\mathtt{y}
. We conclude that
s(\mathtt{x})\sim s(\mathtt{y})
, and thus
(s,h)\models\mathtt{x}\sim\mathtt{y}
.

case:
L\ =\ \mathtt{alloc}(\mathtt{x})
:

If
\mathtt{alloc}(\mathtt{x})\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\varphi
, then
(s,h_{2})\models\mathtt{alloc}(\mathtt{x})
, which implies
s(\mathtt{x})\in{\rm dom}(h)
directly from
h_{2}{\sqsubseteq}h
. Thus,
(s,h)\models\mathtt{alloc}(\mathtt{x})
.
Otherwise, if
\mathtt{alloc}(\mathtt{x})\not\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\varphi
then, since
\varphi
is a core type in
\mathsf{CoreTypes}(\mathtt{X},\alpha)
,
we have
\lnot\mathtt{alloc}(\mathtt{x})\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\varphi
.
By definition of
{\langle\mbox{$\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}$}\rangle}(\varphi,\psi)
, we derive that
\mathtt{alloc}(\mathtt{x})\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}{\langle\mbox{$\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}$}\rangle}(\varphi,\psi)
.
So,
(s,h_{1})\models\mathtt{alloc}(\mathtt{x})
and thus, by
h_{1}{\sqsubseteq}h
,
s(\mathtt{x})\in{\rm dom}(h)
.
We conclude that
(s,h)\models\mathtt{alloc}(\mathtt{x})
.

case:
L\ =\ \lnot\mathtt{alloc}(\mathtt{x})
:

In this case, by definition of
{\langle\mbox{$\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}$}\rangle}(\varphi,\psi)
, we have

\lnot\mathtt{alloc}(\mathtt{x})\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}{\langle\mbox{$\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}$}\rangle}(\varphi,\psi)
,
which implies
(s,h_{1})\models\lnot\mathtt{alloc}(\mathtt{x})
.

Ad absurdum
, suppose
(s,h_{2})\models\mathtt{alloc}(\mathtt{x})
.
Since
\varphi
is a core type in
\mathsf{CoreTypes}(\mathtt{X},\alpha)
,
we conclude that
\mathtt{alloc}(\mathtt{x})\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\varphi
.
However, by definition of
{\langle\mbox{$\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}$}\rangle}(\varphi,\psi)
,
this implies
\mathtt{x}\neq\mathtt{x}\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}{\langle\mbox{$\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}$}\rangle}(\varphi,\psi)
,
which contradicts the fact that
{\langle\mbox{$\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}$}\rangle}(\varphi,\psi)
is satisfiable.
Thus,
(s,h_{2})\models\lnot\mathtt{alloc}(\mathtt{x})
,
which implies
s(\mathtt{x})\not\in{\rm dom}(h_{2})
.
From
h=h_{1}{+}h_{2}
and
s(\mathtt{x})\not\in{\rm dom}(h_{1})
we conclude that
s(\mathtt{x})\not\in{\rm dom}(h)
.
So,
(s,h)\models\lnot\mathtt{alloc}(\mathtt{x})
.

case:
L\ =\ \mathtt{x}\hookrightarrow\mathtt{y}
:

If
\lnot\mathtt{alloc}(\mathtt{x})\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\varphi
, then
{\mathtt{x}\hookrightarrow\mathtt{y}\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}}{\langle\mbox{$\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}$}\rangle}(\varphi,\psi)
holds by definition of
{\langle\mbox{$\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}$}\rangle}(\varphi,\psi)
.
So,
h_{1}(s(\mathtt{x}))=s(\mathtt{y})
and,
from
h_{1}{\sqsubseteq}h
we conclude that
(s,h)\models\mathtt{x}\hookrightarrow\mathtt{y}
.
Otherwise, let us assume that
\mathtt{alloc}(\mathtt{x})\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\varphi
.

Ad absurdum
, suppose
\lnot\mathtt{x}\hookrightarrow\mathtt{y}\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\varphi
.
Then, by definition of
{\langle\mbox{$\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}$}\rangle}(\varphi,\psi)
,
we derive
\mathtt{x}\neq\mathtt{x}\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}{\langle\mbox{$\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}$}\rangle}(\varphi,\psi)
.
However, this contradicts the satisfiability of
{\langle\mbox{$\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}$}\rangle}(\varphi,\psi)
.
Therefore,
\lnot\mathtt{x}\hookrightarrow\mathtt{y}\not\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\varphi
.
Since
\varphi
is a core type, this implies

\mathtt{x}\hookrightarrow\mathtt{y}\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\varphi
,
and therefore
h_{2}(s(\mathtt{x}))=s(\mathtt{y})
.
From
h_{2}{\sqsubseteq}h

we conclude that
(s,h)\models\mathtt{x}\hookrightarrow\mathtt{y}
.

case:
L\ =\ \lnot\mathtt{x}\hookrightarrow\mathtt{y}
:

By definition of
{\langle\mbox{$\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}$}\rangle}(\mathtt{x},\mathtt{y})
,
we have
\lnot\mathtt{x}\hookrightarrow\mathtt{y}\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}{\langle\mbox{$\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}$}\rangle}(\mathtt{x},\mathtt{y})
,
which implies that if
s(\mathtt{x})\in{\rm dom}(h_{1})
then

h_{1}(s(\mathtt{x}))\neq s(\mathtt{y})
.

Ad absurdum
, suppose
\mathtt{x}\hookrightarrow\mathtt{y}\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\varphi
.
Then, by definition of
{\langle\mbox{$\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}$}\rangle}(\varphi,\psi)
,
we derive
\mathtt{x}\neq\mathtt{x}\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}{\langle\mbox{$\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}$}\rangle}(\varphi,\psi)
.
However, this contradicts the satisfiability of
{\langle\mbox{$\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}$}\rangle}(\varphi,\psi)
.
Therefore
\mathtt{x}\hookrightarrow\mathtt{y}\not\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\varphi
and,
since
\varphi
is a core type,
\lnot\mathtt{x}\hookrightarrow\mathtt{y}\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\varphi
.
So, if
s(\mathtt{x})\in{\rm dom}(h_{2})
then

h_{2}(s(\mathtt{x}))\neq s(\mathtt{y})
.
By
h=h_{1}+h_{2}
and the fact that
h_{1}(s(\mathtt{x}))\neq s(\mathtt{y})
, we conclude that
(s,h)\models\lnot\mathtt{x}\hookrightarrow\mathtt{y}
.

case:
L\ =\ \mathtt{size}\geq\beta_{2}
:

If
\mathtt{size}\geq\alpha\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\varphi
, then
{\rm card}({\rm dom}(h))\geq{\rm card}({\rm dom}(h_{2}))\geq\alpha
, by
h_{2}{\sqsubseteq}h
.
As
\beta_{2}\in[0,\alpha]
, this implies
(s,h)\models\mathtt{size}\geq\beta_{2}
.
Otherwise, assume
\mathtt{size}\geq\alpha\not\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\varphi
.
In particular, since
\varphi
is in
\mathsf{CoreTypes}(\mathtt{X},\alpha)
,
this implies that
\max_{\mathtt{size}}(\varphi)<\alpha
and

\textstyle\mathtt{size}\geq\max_{\mathtt{size}}(\varphi)\land\lnot\mathtt{size}\geq\max_{\mathtt{size}}(\varphi)+1\ \subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\ \varphi.

We have
{\rm card}({\rm dom}(h_{2}))=\max_{\mathtt{size}}(\varphi)
.
If
\max_{\mathtt{size}}(\varphi)\geq\beta_{2}
, then from
h_{2}{\sqsubseteq}h

we conclude that
(s,h)\models\mathtt{size}\geq\beta_{2}
.
Otherwise, let us assume
\beta_{2}>\max_{\mathtt{size}}(\varphi)
.
By definition of
{\langle\mbox{$\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}$}\rangle}(\varphi,\psi)
,
we conclude that

\mathtt{size}\geq\beta_{2}+1\mathbin{\text{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raise 4.30554pt\hbox{.}\cr$-$\cr}}}}(\max_{\mathtt{size}}(\varphi)+1)\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}{\langle\mbox{$\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}$}\rangle}(\varphi,\psi)
.
Together with
\beta_{2}>\max_{\mathtt{size}}(\varphi)
, this implies

{\rm card}({\rm dom}(h_{1}))\geq\beta_{2}-\max_{\mathtt{size}}(\varphi)
.
With
{\rm card}({\rm dom}(h_{2}))=\max_{\mathtt{size}}(\varphi)
and
h=h_{1}{+}h_{2}
,
this implies
(s,h)\models\mathtt{size}\geq\beta_{2}
.

case:
L\ =\ \lnot\mathtt{size}\geq\beta_{2}
:

Ad absurdum
, suppose
that
\mathtt{size}\geq\alpha\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\varphi
. Then, by definition of

{\langle\mbox{$\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}$}\rangle}(\varphi,\psi)
we have
\lnot\mathtt{size}\geq\beta_{2}\mathbin{\text{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raise 4.30554pt\hbox{.}\cr$-$\cr}}}}\alpha\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}{\langle\mbox{$\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}$}\rangle}(\varphi,\psi)
.
However, since
\beta_{2}\in[0,\alpha]
,
this means that
\lnot\mathtt{size}\geq 0\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}{\langle\mbox{$\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}$}\rangle}(\varphi,\psi)
,
which contradicts the satisfiability of
{\langle\mbox{$\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}$}\rangle}(\varphi,\psi)
.
Therefore,
\mathtt{size}\geq\alpha\not\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\varphi
.
As
\varphi
is in
\mathsf{CoreTypes}(\mathtt{X},\alpha)
,
we derive
\max_{\mathtt{size}}(\varphi)<\alpha
and

\textstyle\mathtt{size}\geq\max_{\mathtt{size}}(\varphi)\land\lnot\mathtt{size}\geq\max_{\mathtt{size}}(\varphi)+1\ \subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\ \varphi.

We conclude that
{\rm card}({\rm dom}(h_{2}))\leq\max_{\mathtt{size}}(\varphi)
.
From
\mathtt{size}\geq\max_{\mathtt{size}}(\varphi)\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\varphi

and by definition of
{\langle\mbox{$\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}$}\rangle}(\varphi,\psi)
,
we conclude that

\textstyle\lnot\mathtt{size}\geq\beta_{2}\mathbin{\text{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raise 4.30554pt\hbox{.}\cr$-$\cr}}}}\max_{\mathtt{size}}(\varphi)\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}{\langle\mbox{$\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}$}\rangle}(\varphi,\psi).

If
\beta_{2}\leq\max_{\mathtt{size}}(\varphi)
, then
\lnot\mathtt{size}\geq 0\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}{\langle\mbox{$\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}$}\rangle}(\varphi,\psi)
, which contradicts the satisfiability of
{\langle\mbox{$\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}$}\rangle}(\varphi,\psi)
.
Therefore,
\beta_{2}>\max_{\mathtt{size}}(\varphi)
.
So,
{\rm card}({\rm dom}(h_{1}))<\beta_{2}-\max_{\mathtt{size}}(\varphi)
.
Together with
{\rm card}({\rm dom}(h_{2}))\leq\max_{\mathtt{size}}(\varphi)
and

h=h_{1}{+}h
, we conclude that

{\rm card}({\rm dom}(h))<\beta_{2}
, and thus

(s,h)\models\lnot\mathtt{size}\geq\beta_{2}
.

Proof 6.3

(Validity of
(\lnot{\langle\mbox{$\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}$}\rangle}(\varphi,\psi))\ast\varphi\Rightarrow\lnot\psi
)
.

Let us assume

(s,h)\models(\neg{\langle\mbox{$\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}$}\rangle}(\varphi,\psi))\ast\varphi
. Consequently, there is a literal
L
of
{\langle\mbox{$\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}$}\rangle}(\varphi,\psi)
such that

(s,h)\models(\neg L)\ast\varphi
holds.
We show that
(s,h)\models\lnot\psi
.
Let
h_{1}
and
h_{2}
be two disjoint heaps such that
h=h_{1}{+}h_{2}
,

(s,h_{1})\models\lnot L
and
(s,h_{2})\models\varphi
.
We perform a case analysis on the shape of
L
. As in the previous part of the proof, recall that
\mathtt{x},\mathtt{y}\in\mathtt{X}
and
\beta_{1},\beta_{2}\in[0,\alpha]
.

case:
L\ =\ \mathtt{x}\neq\mathtt{x}
:

Since
\varphi
and
\psi
are satisfiable, by definition of
{\langle\mbox{$\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}$}\rangle}(\varphi,\psi)
, the fact that

\mathtt{x}\neq\mathtt{x}\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}{\langle\mbox{$\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}$}\rangle}(\varphi,\psi)
implies that one of the following three cases holds:

1::

\mathtt{alloc}(\mathtt{x})\wedge\neg\mathtt{x}\hookrightarrow\mathtt{y}\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\varphi
and
\mathtt{x}\hookrightarrow\mathtt{y}\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\psi
.

From
\mathtt{alloc}(\mathtt{x})\wedge\neg\mathtt{x}\hookrightarrow\mathtt{y}\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\varphi
and
h_{2}{\sqsubseteq}h
, we have
s(\mathtt{x})\in{\rm dom}(h)

and
h(s(\mathtt{x}))\neq s(\mathtt{y})
.
Thus
(s,h)\not\models\mathtt{x}\hookrightarrow\mathtt{y}
, and so, by
\mathtt{x}\hookrightarrow\mathtt{y}\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\psi
,

(s,h)\models\lnot\psi
.

2::

\mathtt{x}\hookrightarrow\mathtt{y}\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\varphi
and
\neg\mathtt{x}\hookrightarrow\mathtt{y}\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\psi
.

From
\mathtt{x}\hookrightarrow\mathtt{y}\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\varphi
and
h_{2}{\sqsubseteq}h
,
h(s(\mathtt{x}))=s(\mathtt{y})
.
Thus
(s,h)\models\mathtt{x}\hookrightarrow\mathtt{y}
and so, by

\neg\mathtt{x}\hookrightarrow\mathtt{y}\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\psi
,
(s,h)\models\lnot\psi
.

3::

\mathtt{alloc}(\mathtt{x})\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\varphi
and
\neg\mathtt{alloc}(\mathtt{x})\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\psi
.

From
\mathtt{alloc}(\mathtt{x})\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\varphi
and
h_{2}{\sqsubseteq}h
,

s(\mathtt{x})\in{\rm dom}(h)
.
Thus
(s,h)\models\mathtt{alloc}(\mathtt{x})
and so,
by
\neg\mathtt{alloc}(\mathtt{x})\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\psi
,

(s,h)\models\lnot\psi
.

case:
L\ =\ \mathtt{x}\sim\mathtt{y}
, where
\sim\in\{=,\neq\}
:

In this case, since
(s,h_{1})\models\lnot L
,
then we have
(s,h)\models\lnot L
.
Now, it cannot be that
L\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\varphi
,
as it would imply
(s,h)\models L
, which is contradictory.
Therefore, by definition of
{\langle\mbox{$\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}$}\rangle}(\varphi,\psi)
,
we must have
L\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\psi
.
This implies
(s,h)\models\lnot\psi
.

case:
L\ =\ \mathtt{alloc}(\mathtt{x})
:

By definition of
{\langle\mbox{$\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}$}\rangle}(\varphi,\psi)
,

\neg\mathtt{alloc}(\mathtt{x})\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\varphi
and

\mathtt{alloc}(\mathtt{x})\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\psi
.
From
(s,h_{1})\models\lnot\mathtt{alloc}(\mathtt{x})

we conclude that
s(\mathtt{x})\not\in{\rm dom}(h_{1})
.
By
\neg\mathtt{alloc}(\mathtt{x})\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\varphi
,
s(\mathtt{x})\not\in{\rm dom}(h_{2})
.
By
h=h_{1}{+}h_{2}
,
s(\mathtt{x})\not\in{\rm dom}(h)
.
As
\mathtt{alloc}(\mathtt{x})\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\psi
,

{(s,h)\models\lnot\psi}
.

case:
L\ =\ \neg\mathtt{alloc}(\mathtt{x})
:

As
(s,h_{1})\models\lnot L
, we have
s(\mathtt{x})\in{\rm dom}(h_{1})
.
According to the definition of
{\langle\mbox{$\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}$}\rangle}(\varphi,\psi)
,
either
\mathtt{alloc}(\mathtt{x})\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\varphi
or
\lnot\mathtt{alloc}(\mathtt{x})\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\psi
. The first case cannot hold, as it implies
s(\mathtt{x})\in{\rm dom}(h_{2})

which contradicts the fact that
h_{1}
and
h_{2}
are disjoint.
In the second case, from
s(\mathtt{x})\in{\rm dom}(h_{1})
and
h_{1}{\sqsubseteq}h
, we have
(s,h)\models\mathtt{alloc}(\mathtt{x})
. So,
(s,h)\models\lnot\psi
.

case:
L\ =\ \mathtt{x}\hookrightarrow\mathtt{y}
:

Then by definition of
{\langle\mbox{$\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}$}\rangle}(\varphi,\psi)
,

\neg\mathtt{alloc}(\mathtt{x})\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\varphi
and
\mathtt{x}\hookrightarrow\mathtt{y}\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\psi
.
From
(s,h_{1})\models\lnot L
,
if
s(\mathtt{x})\in{\rm dom}(h_{1})
then
h_{1}(s(\mathtt{x}))\neq s(\mathtt{y})
.
As
\neg\mathtt{alloc}(\mathtt{x})\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\varphi
,
s(\mathtt{x})\not\in{\rm dom}(h_{2})
and therefore, by
h=h_{1}{+}h_{2}
,
h(s(\mathtt{x}))\neq s(\mathtt{y})
.
From
\mathtt{x}\hookrightarrow\mathtt{y}\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\psi
,
we conclude that
(s,h)\models\lnot\psi
.

case:
L\ =\ \neg\mathtt{x}\hookrightarrow\mathtt{y}
:

Then, by definition of
{\langle\mbox{$\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}$}\rangle}(\varphi,\psi)
,
\neg\mathtt{x}\hookrightarrow\mathtt{y}\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\psi
.
From
(s,h_{1})\models\lnot L
and
h_{1}{\sqsubseteq}h
,
we derive
h(s(\mathtt{x}))=s(\mathtt{y})
.
From
\neg\mathtt{x}\hookrightarrow\mathtt{y}\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\psi
,
we derive
(s,h)\models\lnot\psi
.

case:
L\ =\ \mathtt{size}\geq\beta_{2}+1\mathbin{\text{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raise 4.44444pt\hbox{.}\cr$-$\cr}}}}\beta_{1}
, where

\mathtt{size}\geq\beta_{2}\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\psi
and
\neg\mathtt{size}\geq\beta_{1}\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\varphi
:

Since it holds that
(s,h_{1})\models\lnot L

and
(s,h_{2})\models\varphi
, we derive (respectively)

{\rm card}({\rm dom}(h_{1}))\leq\beta_{2}\mathbin{\text{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raise 4.30554pt\hbox{.}\cr$-$\cr}}}}\beta_{1}

and
{{\rm card}({\rm dom}(h_{2}))<\beta_{1}}
.
From
h=h_{1}{+}h_{2}
, we conclude that
{\rm card}({\rm dom}(h))<\beta_{2}
.
From
\mathtt{size}\geq\beta_{2}\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\psi
,
we derive
(s,h)\models\lnot\psi
.

case:
L\ =\ \neg\mathtt{size}\geq\beta_{2}\mathbin{\text{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raise 4.44444pt\hbox{.}\cr$-$\cr}}}}\beta_{1}
, where

\neg\mathtt{size}\geq\beta_{2}\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\psi
and
\mathtt{size}\geq\beta_{1}\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\varphi
:

Since we have

(s,h_{1})\models\lnot L
and

(s,h_{2})\models\varphi
, we conclude that

{\rm card}({\rm dom}(h_{1}))\geq\beta_{2}\mathbin{\text{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raise 4.30554pt\hbox{.}\cr$-$\cr}}}}\beta_{1}

and
{\rm card}({\rm dom}(h_{2}))\geq\beta_{1}
.
So,
h=h_{1}{+}h_{2}

implies
{\rm card}({\rm dom}(h))\geq\beta_{2}
.
By
\neg\mathtt{size}\geq\beta_{2}\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\psi
,
we derive
(s,h)\models\lnot\psi
.

We are now ready to tackle the proof of Lemma
13
.

Proof 6.4

(Proof of Lemma
13
)
.

As in the statement of the lemma, let us consider

\mathtt{X}\subseteq_{\mathsf{fin}}\mathsf{VAR}
and
\alpha\geq{\rm card}(\mathtt{X})
,
and two core types

\varphi
and
\psi
in
\mathsf{CoreTypes}(\mathtt{X},\alpha)
.
We want to show that there is a conjunction

\chi\in\mathsf{Conj}(\mathsf{Core}(\mathtt{X},\alpha))

such that
\vdash_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathsf{C}}(\ast,\mathbin{-\mkern-6.0mu*})}(\varphi\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}\psi)\Leftrightarrow\chi
.

First of all,
if
\varphi
or
\psi
is unsatisfiable, then
\vdash_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathsf{C}}(\ast,\mathbin{-\mkern-6.0mu*})}\varphi\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}\psi\Rightarrow\bot
by using Lemma
3

and the admissible axioms
(

I

-
⁣
∗

⁢

.7

)
and
(

I

-
⁣
∗

⁢

.9

)
from Lemma
14
.
Therefore, in this case, it is enough to take
\chi
equal to
\lnot\mathtt{x}=\mathtt{x}
to complete the proof.
Otherwise, let us assume that
\varphi
and
\psi
are satisfiable.
We consider
\chi\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mbox{\tiny def}}}{{=}}{\langle\mbox{$\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}$}\rangle}(\varphi,\psi)
(see Figure
8
), and show that

\vdash_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathsf{C}}(\ast,\mathbin{-\mkern-6.0mu*})}(\varphi\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}\psi)\Leftrightarrow{\langle\mbox{$\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}$}\rangle}(\varphi,\psi)
. We derive each implication separately.

(
\Rightarrow
):

Given Lemma
15
, the proof of
\vdash_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathsf{C}}(\ast,\mathbin{-\mkern-6.0mu*})}\varphi\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}\psi\Rightarrow{\langle\mbox{$\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}$}\rangle}(\varphi,\psi)
is straightforward:

1

\neg{\langle\mbox{$\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}$}\rangle}(\varphi,\psi)\ast\varphi\Rightarrow\neg\psi

Lemma
15
, Theorem
11

2

\neg{\langle\mbox{$\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}$}\rangle}(\varphi,\psi)\Rightarrow(\varphi\mathbin{-\mkern-6.0mu*}\neg\psi)

7
, 1

3

\neg(\varphi\mathbin{-\mkern-6.0mu*}\neg\psi)\Rightarrow{\langle\mbox{$\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}$}\rangle}(\varphi,\psi)

PC, 2

4

(\varphi\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}\psi)\Rightarrow{\langle\mbox{$\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}$}\rangle}(\varphi,\psi)

Def. of
\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}
, 3

(
\Leftarrow
):

Let us now show that
\vdash_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathsf{C}}(\ast,\mathbin{-\mkern-6.0mu*})}{\langle\mbox{$\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}$}\rangle}(\varphi,\psi)\Rightarrow\varphi\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}\psi
.
First, let us note that, since
{\langle\mbox{$\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}$}\rangle}(\varphi,\psi)\ast\varphi\Rightarrow\psi

is valid (Lemma
15
),
it is derivable in
\mathcal{H}_{\mathsf{C}}(\ast){}
(Theorem
11
), and therefore, by the rule
7
,

\vdash_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathsf{C}}(\ast,\mathbin{-\mkern-6.0mu*})}{\langle\mbox{$\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}$}\rangle}(\varphi,\psi)\Rightarrow\varphi\mathbin{-\mkern-6.0mu*}\psi
. From that, it follows that it is enough to show that

{\langle\mbox{$\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}$}\rangle}(\varphi,\psi)\Rightarrow\varphi\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}\top
is derivable in
\mathcal{H}_{\mathsf{C}}(\ast,\mathbin{-\mkern-6.0mu*})
. Indeed,
from
{\langle\mbox{$\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}$}\rangle}(\varphi,\psi)\Rightarrow\varphi\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}\top
and
{\langle\mbox{$\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}$}\rangle}(\varphi,\psi)\Rightarrow\varphi\mathbin{-\mkern-6.0mu*}\psi
, we get, by
(

I

-
⁣
∗

⁢

.17

)
,
that
{\langle\mbox{$\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}$}\rangle}(\varphi,\psi)\Rightarrow\varphi\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}\psi
is derivable too.

Thus, let us prove that
{\langle\mbox{$\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}$}\rangle}(\varphi,\psi)\Rightarrow\varphi\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}\top
is derivable.
If
{\langle\mbox{$\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}$}\rangle}(\varphi,\psi)
is unsatisfiable, then
from the completeness of
\mathcal{H}_{\mathsf{C}}
with respect to Boolean combinations of core formulae (Theorem
5
),
we conclude that
\vdash_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathsf{C}}}{\langle\mbox{$\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}$}\rangle}(\varphi,\psi)\Rightarrow\perp
.
Since
\mathcal{H}_{\mathsf{C}}(\ast,\mathbin{-\mkern-6.0mu*})
extends
\mathcal{H}_{\mathsf{C}}
, we have

\vdash_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathsf{C}}(\ast,\mathbin{-\mkern-6.0mu*})}{\langle\mbox{$\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}$}\rangle}(\varphi,\psi)\Rightarrow\perp
.
By propositional reasoning,
\vdash_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathsf{C}}(\ast,\mathbin{-\mkern-6.0mu*})}{\langle\mbox{$\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}$}\rangle}(\varphi,\psi)\Rightarrow\varphi\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}\top
.
Otherwise, let us assume that
{\langle\mbox{$\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}$}\rangle}(\varphi,\psi)
is satisfiable.

Proof 6.5

(Structure of the remaining part of the proof)
.

Before presenting the technical arguments
for the derivation of
\vdash_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathsf{C}}(\ast,\mathbin{-\mkern-6.0mu*})}{\langle\mbox{$\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}$}\rangle}(\varphi,\psi)\Rightarrow\varphi\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}\top
when
{\langle\mbox{$\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}$}\rangle}(\varphi,\psi)
is satisfiable,
let us explain what are the main
ingredients. The proof establishing that

\vdash_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathsf{C}}(\ast,\mathbin{-\mkern-6.0mu*})}{\langle\mbox{$\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}$}\rangle}(\varphi,\psi)\Rightarrow\varphi\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}\top

is by induction on the number
j
of variables
\mathtt{x}\in\mathtt{X}
for which

\mathtt{alloc}(\mathtt{x})\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\varphi
holds.
As
\varphi
,
\psi
and
{\langle\mbox{$\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}$}\rangle}(\varphi,\psi)
are currently assumed to be
satisfiable, they
have exactly the same equalities and inequalities and this is used in the proof.
The base case
j=0
can be handled using several derivations taking advantage
of Lemma
14
.
For the induction step
j>0
, some more substantial work is needed and this is briefly described below.
We distinguish the case
\max_{\mathtt{size}}(\varphi)<\alpha
from the case
\max_{\mathtt{size}}(\varphi)=\alpha
.
Both cases, we introduce the formula
\textit{\small{Atom}}(\mathtt{x}_{i})
where

\mathtt{alloc}(\mathtt{x}_{i})\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\varphi
.

\textit{\small{Atom}}(\mathtt{x}_{i})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mbox{\tiny def}}}{{=}}\begin{cases}\mathtt{x}_{i}\hookrightarrow\mathtt{y}\land\mathtt{size}=1&\text{if}~\mathtt{x}_{i}\hookrightarrow\mathtt{y}\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\varphi,\text{ for some }\mathtt{y}\in\mathtt{X}\\
\mathtt{alloc}(\mathtt{x}_{i})\land\mathtt{size}=1\land\bigwedge_{\mathtt{y}\in\mathtt{X}}\lnot\mathtt{x}_{i}\hookrightarrow\mathtt{y}&\text{otherwise}\end{cases}

In the case
\max_{\mathtt{size}}(\varphi)<\alpha
, we introduce a formula
\varphi^{\prime}
as a very slight variant
of
\varphi
such that
\varphi^{\prime}
enjoys the following essential properties.

A. :

\varphi^{\prime}
is a satisfiable core type in
\mathsf{CoreTypes}(\mathtt{X},\alpha)
.

B. :

(\textit{\small{Atom}}(\mathtt{x}_{i})\ast\varphi^{\prime})\Rightarrow\varphi
is valid.

C. :

({\langle\mbox{$\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}$}\rangle}(\varphi,\psi)\ast\textit{\small{Atom}}(\mathtt{x}_{i}))\Rightarrow{\langle\mbox{$\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}$}\rangle}(\varphi^{\prime},\psi)
is valid.

In order to conclude
\vdash_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathsf{C}}(\ast,\mathbin{-\mkern-6.0mu*})}{\langle\mbox{$\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}$}\rangle}(\varphi,\psi)\Rightarrow(\varphi\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}\top)
,
we take advantage of the completeness of
\mathcal{H}_{\mathsf{C}}(\ast)

to derive the tautologies in
B. :

and
C. :
.
Moreover, as by construction of
\varphi^{\prime}
, we have
\lnot\mathtt{alloc}(\mathtt{x}_{i})\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\varphi^{\prime}
and,
for every
\mathtt{y}\in\mathtt{X}
,
\lnot\mathtt{alloc}(\mathtt{y})\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\varphi
implies

\lnot\mathtt{alloc}(\mathtt{y})\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\varphi^{\prime}
, we shall be able to apply the
induction hypothesis on
\varphi^{\prime}
to
get

\vdash_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathsf{C}}(\ast,\mathbin{-\mkern-6.0mu*})}{\langle\mbox{$\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}$}\rangle}(\varphi^{\prime},\psi)\Rightarrow(\varphi^{\prime}\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}\top)
, which will be essential in the final derivation for

{\langle\mbox{$\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}$}\rangle}(\varphi,\psi)\Rightarrow(\varphi\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}\top)
.

In the remaining case
\max_{\mathtt{size}}(\varphi)=\alpha
,
we are still looking for some formula
\varphi^{\prime}

such that
\varphi^{\prime}\ast\textit{\small{Atom}}(\mathtt{x}_{i})\Rightarrow\varphi
is valid
but we cannot hope for
\varphi^{\prime}
to be a core type in
\mathsf{CoreTypes}(\mathtt{X},\alpha)
.
Instead, we introduce two core types
\varphi_{\alpha}^{\prime}
and
\varphi_{\alpha-1}^{\prime}
,
and define
\varphi^{\prime}
as
\varphi_{\alpha}^{\prime}\lor\varphi_{\alpha-1}^{\prime}
.
The only difference between
\varphi_{\alpha}^{\prime}
and
\varphi_{\alpha-1}^{\prime}
rests on the fact
that
\mathtt{size}\geq\alpha\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\varphi_{\alpha}^{\prime}
whereas
\neg\mathtt{size}\geq\alpha\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\varphi_{\alpha-1}^{\prime}

(both formulae contain
\mathtt{size}\geq\alpha-1
).
Similarly to the previous case, the properties below shall be shown.

D. :

\varphi_{\alpha}^{\prime}
and
\varphi_{\alpha-1}^{\prime}
are satisfiable core types in
\mathsf{CoreTypes}(\mathtt{X},\alpha)

E. :

(\textit{\small{Atom}}(\mathtt{x}_{i})\ast(\varphi_{\alpha}^{\prime}\lor\varphi_{\alpha-1}^{\prime}))\Rightarrow\varphi
is valid.

F. :

({\langle\mbox{$\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}$}\rangle}(\varphi,\psi)\ast\textit{\small{Atom}}(\mathtt{x}_{i}))\Rightarrow{\langle\mbox{$\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}$}\rangle}(\varphi_{\alpha}^{\prime},\psi)\lor{\langle\mbox{$\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}$}\rangle}(\varphi_{\alpha-1}^{\prime},\psi)
is valid.

The derivation of
\vdash_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathsf{C}}(\ast,\mathbin{-\mkern-6.0mu*})}{\langle\mbox{$\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}$}\rangle}(\varphi,\psi)\Rightarrow(\varphi\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}\top)
follows then
a principle similar to one for the case
\max_{\mathtt{size}}(\varphi)<\alpha
.

Now, let us present the technical developments.
Directly from the definition of
{\langle\mbox{$\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}$}\rangle}(\varphi,\psi)
, the following simple facts hold.

1.

\varphi
,
\psi
and
{\langle\mbox{$\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}$}\rangle}(\varphi,\psi)
have exactly the same equalities and inequalities.

2.

\neg\mathtt{size}\geq 0
is not part of
{\langle\mbox{$\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}$}\rangle}(\varphi,\psi)
,
and therefore, following the definition of
{\langle\mbox{$\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}$}\rangle}(\varphi,\psi)
,
there are no
\mathtt{size}\geq\beta_{1}\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\varphi
and
\neg\mathtt{size}\geq\beta_{2}\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\psi

with
\beta_{1}\geq\beta_{2}
.

3.

\mathtt{x}\neq\mathtt{x}
does not belong to
{\langle\mbox{$\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}$}\rangle}(\varphi,\psi)
.
In particular, by definition of
{\langle\mbox{$\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}$}\rangle}(\varphi,\psi)
, none of the following conditions apply:

–

there is
\mathtt{x}\in\mathtt{X}
such that
\mathtt{alloc}(\mathtt{x})\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\varphi
and
\lnot\mathtt{alloc}(\mathtt{x})\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\psi
,

–

there are
\mathtt{x},\mathtt{y}\in\mathtt{X}
such that
\mathtt{x}\hookrightarrow\mathtt{y}\in\varphi
and
\lnot\mathtt{x}\hookrightarrow\mathtt{y}\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\psi
,

–

there are
\mathtt{x},\mathtt{y}\in\mathtt{X}
such that
\mathtt{alloc}(\mathtt{x})\land\lnot\mathtt{x}\hookrightarrow\mathtt{y}\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\varphi
and
\mathtt{x}\hookrightarrow\mathtt{y}\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\psi
.

From
1.
, we know that
{\langle\mbox{$\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}$}\rangle}(\varphi,\psi)
and
\varphi
satisfy the same (in)equalities.
Similarly to the proof of Lemma
9
,
let
\mathtt{x}_{1},\ldots\mathtt{x}_{n}
be a maximal enumeration of representatives of the
equivalence classes (one per equivalence class) such that
\mathtt{alloc}(\mathtt{x}_{i})
occurs in
\varphi
.
As it is maximal, for every
\mathtt{alloc}(\mathtt{x})
in
\mathsf{Lt}(\varphi)
there is
i\in[1,n]
such that
\mathtt{x}_{i}
is syntactically equal to
\mathtt{x}
.
Moreover, by definition of
{\langle\mbox{$\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}$}\rangle}(\varphi,\psi)
, for every
i\in[1,n]
,

\lnot\mathtt{alloc}(\mathtt{x}_{i})\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}{\langle\mbox{$\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}$}\rangle}(\varphi,\psi)
.
The proof of
\vdash_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathsf{C}}(\ast,\mathbin{-\mkern-6.0mu*})}{\langle\mbox{$\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}$}\rangle}(\varphi,\psi)\Rightarrow\varphi\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}\top
is by induction on the number
j
of variables
\mathtt{x}\in\mathtt{X}
for which
\mathtt{alloc}(\mathtt{x})\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\varphi
holds.

base case:
j=0
:

In the base case, no formula
\mathtt{alloc}(\mathtt{x})
occurs positively in
\varphi
. Since
\varphi
is a core type, this implies that for every
\mathtt{x}\in\mathtt{X}
,
\lnot\mathtt{alloc}(\mathtt{x})\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\varphi
. Moreover,
since
\varphi
is satisfiable, for every
\mathtt{x},\mathtt{y}\in\mathtt{X}
,

\lnot\mathtt{x}\hookrightarrow\mathtt{y}\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\varphi
(see the axiom
(

A
C

5

)
).
Therefore, the core type
\varphi
is syntactically equivalent (up to associativity and commutativity of conjunction) to the formula

\varphi_{\mathtt{size}}\land\varphi_{\lnot\texttt{alloc}}\land\varphi_{\not\hookrightarrow}\land\varphi_{\texttt{(in)eq}},

where

•:

\varphi_{\mathtt{size}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mbox{\tiny def}}}{{=}}\bigwedge\big(\{\mathtt{size}\geq\beta\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\varphi\}\cup\{\lnot\mathtt{size}\geq\beta\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\varphi\}\big)
,

•:

\varphi_{\lnot\mathtt{alloc}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mbox{\tiny def}}}{{=}}\bigwedge_{\mathtt{x}\in\mathtt{X}}\lnot\mathtt{alloc}(\mathtt{x})
,

•:

\varphi_{\not\hookrightarrow}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mbox{\tiny def}}}{{=}}\bigwedge_{\mathtt{x},\mathtt{y}\in\mathtt{X}}\lnot\mathtt{x}\hookrightarrow\mathtt{y}
,

•:

\varphi_{\texttt{(in)eq}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mbox{\tiny def}}}{{=}}\bigwedge\{\mathtt{x}\sim\mathtt{y}\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\varphi\mid\sim\in\{=,\neq\}\}
.

Since
\varphi
is satisfiable, so is
\varphi_{\mathtt{size}}
. We show that

\vdash_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathsf{C}}(\ast,\mathbin{-\mkern-6.0mu*})}(\varphi_{\mathtt{size}}\land\varphi_{\lnot\texttt{alloc}}\land\varphi_{\not\hookrightarrow})\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}\top
:

1

\varphi_{\mathtt{size}}\land\varphi_{\lnot\texttt{alloc}}\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}\top

(

I

-
⁣
∗

⁢

.23

)

2

\lnot\mathtt{alloc}(\mathtt{x})\Rightarrow\lnot\mathtt{x}\hookrightarrow\mathtt{y}

(

A
C

5

)
, PC

3

\varphi_{\lnot\texttt{alloc}}\Rightarrow\varphi_{\not\hookrightarrow}

PC, repeated 2

4

\varphi_{\mathtt{size}}\land\varphi_{\lnot\texttt{alloc}}\Rightarrow\varphi_{\mathtt{size}}\land\varphi_{\lnot\texttt{alloc}}\land\varphi_{\not\hookrightarrow}

PC, 3

, 4

6

\varphi_{\mathtt{size}}\land\varphi_{\lnot\texttt{alloc}}\land\varphi_{\not\hookrightarrow}\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}\top

Modus Ponens, 1, 5

Now, let us treat the formula
\varphi_{\texttt{(in)eq}}
. From the definition of
{\langle\mbox{$\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}$}\rangle}(\varphi,\psi)
, we have
\varphi_{\texttt{(in)eq}}\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}{\langle\mbox{$\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}$}\rangle}(\varphi,\psi)
, and so
by propositional reasoning,
\vdash_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathsf{C}}(\ast,\mathbin{-\mkern-6.0mu*})}{\langle\mbox{$\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}$}\rangle}(\varphi,\psi)\Rightarrow\varphi_{\texttt{(in)eq}}
.
This allows us to conclude that

\vdash_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathsf{C}}(\ast,\mathbin{-\mkern-6.0mu*})}{\langle\mbox{$\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}$}\rangle}(\varphi,\psi)\Rightarrow\big((\varphi_{\mathtt{size}}\land\varphi_{\lnot\texttt{alloc}}\land\varphi_{\not\hookrightarrow}\land\varphi_{\texttt{(in)eq}})\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}\top\big),

(
\dagger
)

by induction on the number of literals
\mathtt{x}\sim\mathtt{y}
appearing in
\varphi_{\texttt{(in)eq}}
,
and by relying on the two theorems
(

.19

(

. In the base case,
\varphi_{\texttt{(in)eq}}=\top
, and so

7

PC

(\varphi_{\mathtt{size}}\land\varphi_{\lnot\texttt{alloc}}\land\varphi_{\not\hookrightarrow}\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}\top)\Rightarrow(\varphi_{\mathtt{size}}\land\varphi_{\lnot\texttt{alloc}}\land\varphi_{\not\hookrightarrow}\land\varphi_{\texttt{(in)eq}}\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}\top)

, 7

\varphi_{\mathtt{size}}\land\varphi_{\lnot\texttt{alloc}}\land\varphi_{\not\hookrightarrow}\land\varphi_{\texttt{(in)eq}}\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}\top

Modus Ponens, 6, 8

{\langle\mbox{$\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}$}\rangle}(\varphi,\psi)\Rightarrow(\varphi_{\mathtt{size}}\land\varphi_{\lnot\texttt{alloc}}\land\varphi_{\not\hookrightarrow}\land\varphi_{\texttt{(in)eq}}\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}\top)

In the induction step, let
\varphi_{\texttt{(in)eq}}=\varphi_{\texttt{(in)eq}}^{\prime}\land\mathtt{x}\sim\mathtt{y}
, where
\mathtt{x}\sim\mathtt{y}\not\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\varphi_{\texttt{(in)eq}}^{\prime}
.

{\langle\mbox{$\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}$}\rangle}(\varphi,\psi)

As announced earlier, we define the formula:

\textit{\small{Atom}}(\mathtt{x}_{i})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mbox{\tiny def}}}{{=}}\begin{cases}\mathtt{x}_{i}\hookrightarrow\mathtt{y}\land\mathtt{size}=1&\text{if}~\mathtt{x}_{i}\hookrightarrow\mathtt{y}\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\varphi,\text{ for some }\mathtt{y}\in\mathtt{X}\\
\mathtt{alloc}(\mathtt{x}_{i})\land\mathtt{size}=1\land\bigwedge_{\mathtt{y}\in\mathtt{X}}\lnot\mathtt{x}_{i}\hookrightarrow\mathtt{y}&\text{otherwise}\end{cases}

Notice that, if there is
\mathtt{y}\in\mathtt{X}

\mathtt{x}_{i}\hookrightarrow\mathtt{y}\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\varphi
