)
, 3, 5, PC

From the hypothesis
{\rm card}(\mathtt{X})\leq\alpha
, together with
\mathtt{alloc}(\mathtt{x}_{i})\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\varphi
and the fact that
\varphi
is satisfiable,
we have
\max_{\mathtt{size}}(\varphi)\geq 1
(see
(

I
C

11

)
, instantiated with
\mathtt{X}=\{\mathtt{x}_{i}\}
).
In order to show that
\vdash_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathsf{C}}{\ast,\mathbin{-\mkern-6.0mu*}}}{\langle\mbox{$\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}$}\rangle}(\varphi,\psi)\Rightarrow(\varphi\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}\top)
,
we split the proof depending on whether
\max_{\mathtt{size}}(\varphi)<\alpha
holds.

case:
\max_{\mathtt{size}}(\varphi)<\alpha
:

Since
\varphi
is a satisfiable core type in
\mathsf{CoreTypes}(\mathtt{X},\alpha)
, by definition of
\max_{\mathtt{size}}(.)
, we have
\mathtt{size}\geq\max_{\mathtt{size}}(\varphi)\land\lnot\mathtt{size}\geq\max_{\mathtt{size}}(\varphi)+1\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\varphi
.
Below, we consider the formula
\varphi^{\prime}
obtained from
\varphi
by:

•:

replacing
\mathtt{size}\geq\max_{\mathtt{size}}(\varphi)\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\varphi
with
\lnot\mathtt{size}\geq\max_{\mathtt{size}}(\varphi)
,

•:

for every
\mathtt{x}\in\mathtt{X}
such that
\mathtt{x}=\mathtt{x}_{i}\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\varphi
, replacing every literal
\mathtt{alloc}(\mathtt{x})\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\varphi
with
\lnot\mathtt{alloc}(\mathtt{x})
, and every literal
\mathtt{x}\hookrightarrow\mathtt{y}\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\varphi
with

\lnot\mathtt{x}\hookrightarrow\mathtt{y}
, where
\mathtt{y}\in\mathtt{X}
.

Explicitly,

\varphi^{\prime}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mbox{\tiny def}}}{{=}}\begin{aligned} &\bigwedge\{\mathtt{x}\sim\mathtt{y}\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\varphi\mid\sim\in\{=,\neq\}\}\land\bigwedge\{\mathtt{alloc}(\mathtt{x})\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\varphi\mid\mathtt{x}\neq\mathtt{x}_{i}\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\varphi\}\land\\
&\bigwedge\{\lnot\mathtt{alloc}(\mathtt{x})\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\varphi\}\land\bigwedge\{\lnot\mathtt{alloc}(\mathtt{x})\mid\mathtt{x}=\mathtt{x}_{i}\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\varphi\}\land\bigwedge\{\mathtt{x}\hookrightarrow\mathtt{y}\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\varphi\mid\mathtt{x}\neq\mathtt{x}_{i}\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\varphi\}\land\\
&\bigwedge\{\lnot\mathtt{x}\hookrightarrow\mathtt{y}\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\varphi\}\land\bigwedge\{\lnot\mathtt{x}\hookrightarrow\mathtt{y}\mid\mathtt{x}=\mathtt{x}_{i}\land\mathtt{x}\hookrightarrow\mathtt{y}\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\varphi\}\land\lnot\mathtt{size}\geq{\textstyle\max_{\mathtt{size}}(\varphi)}\land\\
&\bigwedge\{\mathtt{size}\geq\beta\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\varphi\mid\beta<{\textstyle\max_{\mathtt{size}}(\varphi)}\}\land\bigwedge\{\lnot\mathtt{size}\geq\beta\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\varphi\}.\end{aligned}

The formula
\varphi^{\prime}
enjoys the following properties (to be shown below):

A. :

\varphi^{\prime}
is a satisfiable core type in
\mathsf{CoreTypes}(\mathtt{X},\alpha)
.

B. :

(\textit{\small{Atom}}(\mathtt{x}_{i})\ast\varphi^{\prime})\Rightarrow\varphi
is valid.

C. :

({\langle\mbox{$\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}$}\rangle}(\varphi,\psi)\ast\textit{\small{Atom}}(\mathtt{x}_{i}))\Rightarrow{\langle\mbox{$\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}$}\rangle}(\varphi^{\prime},\psi)
is valid.

Fundamentally,
\varphi^{\prime}
enjoys the induction hypothesis, which reveals to be useful
later on.

Proof 6.6

(Proof of
A. :
)
.

Since
\varphi^{\prime}
is obtained from
\varphi
simply by changing the polarity of some of the literals in
\mathsf{Lt}(\varphi)
, clearly
\varphi^{\prime}
is in
\mathsf{CoreTypes}(\mathtt{X},\alpha)
. To show that
\varphi^{\prime}
is satisfiable, we rely on the fact that
\varphi
is satisfiable. Let
(s,h)
be a memory state satisfying
\varphi
.
Since
\mathtt{alloc}(\mathtt{x}_{i})\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\varphi
, we conclude that
s(\mathtt{x}_{i})\in{\rm dom}(h)
.
Let us consider the disjoint heaps
h_{1}
and
h_{2}
such that

h=h_{1}+h_{2}
and
{\rm dom}(h_{1})=\{s(\mathtt{x}_{i})\}
.
We show that
(s,h_{2})\models\varphi^{\prime}
by
considering every
L\in\mathsf{Lt}(\varphi^{\prime})
and showing that

(s,h_{2})\models L
.

case:
L\,=\,\mathtt{x}\sim\mathtt{y}
, where
\sim\in\{=,\neq\}
:

By definition of
\varphi^{\prime}
,
(s,h)\models L
and therefore
s(\mathtt{x})\sim s(\mathtt{y})
.
Thus,
(s,h_{2})\models L
.

case:
L\,=\,\lnot\mathtt{alloc}(\mathtt{x})
:

If
\mathtt{x}=\mathtt{x}_{i}\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\varphi
then
s(\mathtt{x})\in{\rm dom}(h_{1})
, and therefore, by
h_{1}\sharp h_{2}
,
s(\mathtt{x})\not\in{\rm dom}(h_{2})
. So,
(s,h_{2})\models\lnot\mathtt{alloc}(\mathtt{x})
.
Otherwise (
\mathtt{x}\neq\mathtt{x}_{i}\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\varphi
), by definition of
\varphi^{\prime}
, we have
\lnot\mathtt{alloc}(\mathtt{x})\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\varphi
.
So
s(\mathtt{x})\not\in{\rm dom}(h)
and, from
h_{2}{\sqsubseteq}h
, we conclude that
(s,h_{2})\models\lnot\mathtt{alloc}(\mathtt{x})
.

case:
L\,=\,\lnot\mathtt{x}\hookrightarrow\mathtt{y}
:

Similar to the previous case. Briefly, if
\mathtt{x}=\mathtt{x}_{i}\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\varphi
then, by definition of
\textit{\small{Atom}}(\mathtt{x}_{i})
,
(s,h_{2})\not\models\mathtt{alloc}(\mathtt{x})
, which implies
(s,h_{2})\models\lnot\mathtt{x}\hookrightarrow\mathtt{y}
.
Otherwise, by definition of
\varphi^{\prime}
,
\lnot\mathtt{x}\hookrightarrow\mathtt{y}\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\varphi
and thus
(s,h)\models\lnot\mathtt{x}\hookrightarrow\mathtt{y}
.
From
h_{2}{\sqsubseteq}h
, we conclude that
(s,h_{2})\models\lnot\mathtt{x}\hookrightarrow\mathtt{y}
.

case:
L\,=\,\mathtt{alloc}(\mathtt{x})
:

By definition of
\varphi^{\prime}
,
\mathtt{alloc}(\mathtt{x})\land\mathtt{x}\neq\mathtt{x}_{i}\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\varphi
.
Therefore
s(\mathtt{x})\in{\rm dom}(h)
and, by definition of
\textit{\small{Atom}}(\mathtt{x}_{i})
,
s(\mathtt{x})\not\in{\rm dom}(h_{1})
.
Since
h=h_{1}{+}h_{2}
, we conclude that
(s,h_{2})\models\mathtt{alloc}(\mathtt{x})
.

case:
L\,=\,\mathtt{x}\hookrightarrow\mathtt{y}
:

Similar to the previous case. By definition of
\varphi^{\prime}
, we have

{\mathtt{x}\hookrightarrow\mathtt{y}}\land\mathtt{x}\neq\mathtt{x}_{i}\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\varphi
.
Thus,
h(s(\mathtt{x}))=s(\mathtt{y})
.
By definition of
\textit{\small{Atom}}(\mathtt{x}_{i})
,

s(\mathtt{x})\in{\rm dom}(h_{2})
and thus

h_{2}(s(\mathtt{x}))=s(\mathtt{y})
.
So,
(s,h_{2})\models\mathtt{x}\hookrightarrow\mathtt{y}
.

case:
L\,=\,\mathtt{size}\geq\beta
:

By definition of
\varphi^{\prime}
,
\beta<\max_{\mathtt{size}}(\varphi)
.
Since
(s,h)\models\varphi
,
we have
{\rm card}({\rm dom}(h))\geq\max_{\mathtt{size}}(\varphi)
.
By definition of
\textit{\small{Atom}}(\mathtt{x}_{i})
and from
h=h_{1}{+}h_{2}
,
we have
{\rm card}({\rm dom}(h_{2}))={\rm card}({\rm dom}(h)){-}1\geq\max_{\mathtt{size}}(\varphi){-}1\geq\beta
.
So,
{(s,h_{2})\models\mathtt{size}\geq\beta}
.

case:
L\,=\,\lnot\mathtt{size}\geq\beta
:

By definition of
\varphi^{\prime}
,

{\lnot\mathtt{size}\geq\beta\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\varphi}

or

{\beta=\max_{\mathtt{size}}(\varphi)}
.
In the former case, since
\varphi
is satisfiable, we know that
\beta>\max_{\mathtt{size}}(\varphi)
. Hence, in both cases we have
\beta\geq\max_{\mathtt{size}}(\varphi)
.
As
(s,h)\models\varphi
and
\lnot\mathtt{size}\geq\max_{\mathtt{size}}(\varphi)+1\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\varphi
, we have

{\rm card}({\rm dom}(h))\leq\max_{\mathtt{size}}(\varphi)
.
Since
{\rm card}({\rm dom}(h_{1}))=1
, by
h=h_{1}{+}h_{2}
we derive
{\rm card}({\rm dom}(h_{2}))<\max_{\mathtt{size}}(\varphi)\leq\beta
.
Therefore,
(s,h_{2})\models\lnot\mathtt{size}\geq\beta
.

Proof 6.7

(Proof of
B. :
)
.

Let
(s,h)\models\textit{\small{Atom}}(\mathtt{x}_{i})\ast\varphi^{\prime}
.
So, there are
h_{1}
and
h_{2}
such that
h=h_{1}{+}h_{2}
,

(s,h_{1})\models\textit{\small{Atom}}(\mathtt{x}_{i})
and
(s,h_{2})\models\varphi^{\prime}
.
By definition of
\textit{\small{Atom}}(\mathtt{x}_{i})
,
{\rm dom}(h_{1})=\{s(\mathtt{x}_{i})\}
.
In order to prove
B. :
,
we show that
(s,h)\models L
, for every literal
L\in\mathsf{Lt}(\varphi)
.

case:
L\,=\,\mathtt{x}\sim\mathtt{y}
, where
\sim\in\{=,\neq\}
:

By definition of
\varphi^{\prime}
,
(s,h_{2})\models L
and therefore
s(\mathtt{x})\sim s(\mathtt{y})
.
Hence,
(s,h)\models L
.

case:
L\,=\,\lnot\mathtt{alloc}(\mathtt{x})
:

By definition of
\textit{\small{Atom}}(\mathtt{x}_{i})
,
\mathtt{alloc}(\mathtt{x}_{i})\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\varphi
and therefore
s(\mathtt{x})\not\in{\rm dom}(h_{1})
.
By definition of
\varphi^{\prime}
, for every
\mathtt{y}\in\mathtt{X}
,
\mathtt{alloc}(\mathtt{y})\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\varphi^{\prime}
implies
\mathtt{alloc}(\mathtt{y})\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\varphi
.
Therefore,
s(\mathtt{x})\not\in{\rm dom}(h_{2})
.
We conclude that
s(\mathtt{x})\not\in{\rm dom}(h)
, and so

(s,h)\models\lnot\mathtt{alloc}(\mathtt{x})
.

case:
L\,=\,\lnot\mathtt{x}\hookrightarrow\mathtt{y}
:

Similar to the previous case. Briefly, by definition of
\textit{\small{Atom}}(\mathtt{x}_{i})
,
(s,h_{1})\models\lnot\mathtt{x}\hookrightarrow\mathtt{y}
.
By definition of
\varphi^{\prime}
,
(s,h_{2})\models\lnot\mathtt{x}\hookrightarrow\mathtt{y}
. So,
(s,h)\models\lnot\mathtt{x}\hookrightarrow\mathtt{y}
.

case:
L\,=\,\mathtt{alloc}(\mathtt{x})
:

If
\mathtt{x}=\mathtt{x}_{i}\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\varphi
, then

s(\mathtt{x})=s(\mathtt{x}_{i})
(first case of the proof),
and by definition of
\textit{\small{Atom}}(\mathtt{x}_{i})
,
s(\mathtt{x})\in{\rm dom}(h_{1})
.
As
h_{1}{\sqsubseteq}h
, we conclude that
(s,h)\models\mathtt{alloc}(\mathtt{x})
.
Otherwise, if
\mathtt{x}\neq\mathtt{x}_{i}\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\varphi
, then by definition of
\varphi^{\prime}
we have
\mathtt{alloc}(\mathtt{x})\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\varphi^{\prime}
.
This implies that
s(\mathtt{x})\in{\rm dom}(h_{2})
and so, from
h_{2}{\sqsubseteq}h
, we conclude that
(s,h)\models\mathtt{alloc}(\mathtt{x})
.

case:
L\,=\,\mathtt{x}\hookrightarrow\mathtt{y}
:

Similar to the previous case. Briefly, if
\mathtt{x}=\mathtt{x}_{i}\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\varphi
then, by definition of
\textit{\small{Atom}}(\mathtt{x}_{i})
,

(s,h_{1})\models\mathtt{x}\hookrightarrow\mathtt{y}
and so

(s,h)\models\mathtt{x}\hookrightarrow\mathtt{y}
.
Else (
{\mathtt{x}\neq\mathtt{x}_{i}}\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\varphi
),

\mathtt{x}\hookrightarrow\mathtt{y}\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\varphi^{\prime}
and therefore

(s,h_{2})\models\mathtt{x}\hookrightarrow\mathtt{y}
.
So,
(s,h)\models\mathtt{x}\hookrightarrow\mathtt{y}
.

case:
L\,=\,\mathtt{size}\geq\beta
:

If
\beta<\max_{\mathtt{size}}(\varphi)
, then directly by definition of
\varphi^{\prime}
, we have
(s,h_{2})\models\mathtt{size}\geq\beta
. From
h_{2}{\sqsubseteq}h
, we conclude that

(s,h)\models\mathtt{size}\geq\beta
.
Otherwise,
\beta=\max_{\mathtt{size}}(\varphi)
. Recall that
\max_{\mathtt{size}}(\varphi)\geq 1
and so,
by definition of
\varphi^{\prime}
,
\mathtt{size}\geq\max_{\mathtt{size}}(\varphi)-1\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\varphi^{\prime}
.
Thus,
{\rm card}({\rm dom}(h_{2}))\geq\max_{\mathtt{size}}(\varphi)-1
.
By definition of
\textit{\small{Atom}}(\mathtt{x}_{i})
we have
{\rm card}({\rm dom}(h_{1}))=1
.
As
h=h_{1}{+}h_{2}
, we conclude that

(s,h)\models\mathtt{size}\geq\max_{\mathtt{size}}(\varphi)
.

case:
L\,=\,\lnot\mathtt{size}\geq\beta
:

As
\varphi
is satisfiable,
\beta>\max_{\mathtt{size}}(\varphi)
.
By definition of the formula
\varphi^{\prime}
,

{\lnot\mathtt{size}\geq\max_{\mathtt{size}}(\varphi)\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\varphi^{\prime}}

and thus
{\rm card}({\rm dom}(h_{2}))<\max_{\mathtt{size}}(\varphi)
.
From
{{\rm card}({\rm dom}(h_{1}))=1}
we can derive
{\rm card}({\rm dom}(h))\leq\max_{\mathtt{size}}(\varphi)<\beta
,
which allows us to conclude that

{(s,h)\models\lnot\mathtt{size}\geq\beta}
.

\begin{array}[]{rll}&\bigwedge{\scalebox{0.8}{$\left\{\mathtt{x}\sim\mathtt{y}\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\{\varphi^{\prime}\mathbin{|}\psi\}\,\middle|\,{{{\begin{array}[]{r}\sim\in\{=,\neq\}\end{array}}}}\right\}$}}&\land\bigwedge{\scalebox{0.8}{$\left\{\mathtt{alloc}(\mathtt{x})\,\middle|\,{{{\begin{array}[]{r}\lnot\mathtt{alloc}(\mathtt{x})\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\varphi^{\prime}\\
\mathtt{alloc}(\mathtt{x})\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\psi\end{array}}}}\right\}$}}\\
\land&\bigwedge{\scalebox{0.8}{$\left\{\lnot\mathtt{alloc}(\mathtt{x})\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\psi\right\}$}}&\land\bigwedge{\scalebox{0.8}{$\left\{\lnot\mathtt{alloc}(\mathtt{x})\,\middle|\,{{{\begin{array}[]{r}\mathtt{alloc}(\mathtt{x})\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\varphi^{\prime}\end{array}}}}\right\}$}}\\
\land&\bigwedge{\scalebox{0.8}{$\left\{\lnot\mathtt{x}{\hookrightarrow}\mathtt{y}\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\psi\right\}$}}&\land\bigwedge{\scalebox{0.8}{$\left\{\mathtt{x}\hookrightarrow\mathtt{y}\,\middle|\,{{{\begin{array}[]{r}\lnot\mathtt{alloc}(\mathtt{x})\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\varphi^{\prime}\\
\mathtt{x}\hookrightarrow\mathtt{y}\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\psi\end{array}}}}\right\}$}}\\
\land&\bigwedge{\scalebox{0.8}{$\left\{\mathtt{x}\neq\mathtt{x}\,\middle|\,{{{\begin{array}[]{r}\mathtt{alloc}(\mathtt{x})\land\lnot\mathtt{x}\hookrightarrow\mathtt{y}\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\varphi^{\prime}\\
\mathtt{x}\hookrightarrow\mathtt{y}\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\psi\end{array}}}}\right\}$}}&\land\bigwedge{\scalebox{0.8}{$\left\{\mathtt{size}\geq\beta_{2}{+}1{\mathbin{\text{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raise 6.19998pt\hbox{.}\cr$-$\cr}}}}}\beta_{1}\,\middle|\,{{{\begin{array}[]{r}\lnot\mathtt{size}\geq\beta_{1}\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\varphi^{\prime}\\
\mathtt{size}\geq\beta_{2}\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\psi\end{array}}}}\right\}$}}\\
\land&\bigwedge{\scalebox{0.8}{$\left\{\mathtt{x}\neq\mathtt{x}\,\middle|\,{{{\begin{array}[]{r}\mathtt{x}\hookrightarrow\mathtt{y}\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\varphi^{\prime}\\
\lnot\mathtt{x}\hookrightarrow\mathtt{y}\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\psi\end{array}}}}\right\}$}}&\land\bigwedge{\scalebox{0.8}{$\left\{\lnot\mathtt{size}\geq\beta_{2}{\mathbin{\text{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raise 6.19998pt\hbox{.}\cr$-$\cr}}}}}\beta_{1}\,\middle|\,{{{\begin{array}[]{r}\mathtt{size}\geq\beta_{1}\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\varphi^{\prime}\\
\lnot\mathtt{size}\geq\beta_{2}\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\psi\end{array}}}}\right\}$}}\\
\land&\bigwedge{\scalebox{0.8}{$\left\{\mathtt{x}\neq\mathtt{x}\,\middle|\,{{{\begin{array}[]{r}\mathtt{alloc}(\mathtt{x})\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\varphi^{\prime}\\
\lnot\mathtt{alloc}(\mathtt{x})\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\psi\end{array}}}}\right\}$}}\end{array}

Figure 9
.
The formula
{\langle\mbox{$\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}$}\rangle}(\varphi^{\prime},\psi)
.

Proof of
C. :
.:

Figure
9
recalls the definition of
{\langle\mbox{$\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}$}\rangle}(\varphi^{\prime},\psi)
.
First of all, notice that
it cannot be that there is
\mathtt{x}\in\mathtt{X}
such that

\mathtt{x}\neq\mathtt{x}\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}{\langle\mbox{$\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}$}\rangle}(\varphi^{\prime},\psi)
.
Indeed,
ad absurdum
, suppose the opposite.
By definition of
{\langle\mbox{$\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}$}\rangle}(\varphi^{\prime},\psi)
, this implies that
(1)
\mathtt{alloc}(\mathtt{x})\land\lnot\mathtt{x}\hookrightarrow\mathtt{y}\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\varphi^{\prime}

and
\mathtt{x}\hookrightarrow\mathtt{y}\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\psi
,
(2)
\mathtt{x}\hookrightarrow\mathtt{y}\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\varphi^{\prime}
and
\lnot\mathtt{x}\hookrightarrow\mathtt{y}\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\psi
, or (3)
\mathtt{alloc}(\mathtt{x})\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\varphi^{\prime}
and
\lnot\mathtt{alloc}(\mathtt{x})\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\psi
.
By definition of
\varphi^{\prime}
, this implies that
(1)
\mathtt{alloc}(\mathtt{x})\land\lnot\mathtt{x}\hookrightarrow\mathtt{y}\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\varphi
,
(2)
\mathtt{x}\hookrightarrow\mathtt{y}\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\varphi

or (3)
\mathtt{alloc}(\mathtt{x})\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\varphi
.
However, by definition of
{\langle\mbox{$\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}$}\rangle}(\varphi,\psi)
,
this implies that
\mathtt{x}\neq\mathtt{x}\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}{\langle\mbox{$\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}$}\rangle}(\varphi,\psi)
,
in contradiction with the satisfiability of
{\langle\mbox{$\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}$}\rangle}(\varphi,\psi)
.
Therefore, below we assume that for all
\mathtt{x}\in\mathtt{X}
,

\mathtt{x}\neq\mathtt{x}\not\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}{\langle\mbox{$\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}$}\rangle}(\varphi^{\prime},\psi)
.

Let
(s,h)\models{\langle\mbox{$\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}$}\rangle}(\varphi,\psi)\ast\textit{\small{Atom}}(\mathtt{x}_{i})
.
There are
h_{1}
and
h_{2}
such that
h=h_{1}{+}h_{2}
,

(s,h_{1})\models{\langle\mbox{$\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}$}\rangle}(\varphi,\psi)
and

(s,h_{2})\models\textit{\small{Atom}}(\mathtt{x}_{i})
.
By definition of
\textit{\small{Atom}}(\mathtt{x}_{i})
,
{\rm dom}(h_{2})=\{s(\mathtt{x}_{i})\}
.
To prove
C. :
,
we show that
(s,h)\models L
,
for every literal
L\in\mathsf{Lt}({\langle\mbox{$\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}$}\rangle}(\varphi^{\prime},\psi))
.

case:
L\,=\,\mathtt{x}\sim\mathtt{y}
, where
\sim\in\{=,\neq\}
:

By definition of
{\langle\mbox{$\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}$}\rangle}(\varphi^{\prime},\psi)
,

L\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\{\varphi^{\prime}\mathbin{|}\psi\}
and so,
by definition of
\varphi^{\prime}
,
L\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\{\varphi\mathbin{|}\psi\}
.
By definition of
{\langle\mbox{$\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}$}\rangle}(\varphi,\psi)
,

L\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}{\langle\mbox{$\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}$}\rangle}(\varphi,\psi)
.
From
(s,h_{1})\models{\langle\mbox{$\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}$}\rangle}(\varphi,\psi)
we derive
s(\mathtt{x})\sim s(\mathtt{y})
.
So,
(s,h)\models L
.

case:
L\,=\,\lnot\mathtt{alloc}(\mathtt{x})
:

From the definition of
{\langle\mbox{$\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}$}\rangle}(\varphi^{\prime},\psi)
,
either we have
\lnot\mathtt{alloc}(\mathtt{x})\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\psi
or we
have
\mathtt{alloc}(\mathtt{x})\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\varphi^{\prime}
.
In the first case, by definition of
{\langle\mbox{$\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}$}\rangle}(\varphi,\psi)
,

\lnot\mathtt{alloc}(\mathtt{x})\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}{\langle\mbox{$\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}$}\rangle}(\varphi,\psi)
, and
therefore
s(\mathtt{x})\not\in{\rm dom}(h_{1})
.
Moreover, since

{\langle\mbox{$\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}$}\rangle}(\varphi,\psi)
is satisfiable,
\mathtt{alloc}(\mathtt{x})\not\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\varphi
(otherwise we would have
\mathtt{x}\neq\mathtt{x}\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}{\langle\mbox{$\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}$}\rangle}(\varphi,\psi)
).
Therefore, by definition of
\textit{\small{Atom}}(\mathtt{x}_{i})
,
we conclude that
s(\mathtt{x})\not\in{\rm dom}(h_{2})
.
From
h=h_{1}{+}h_{2}
, we derive

s(\mathtt{x})\not\in{\rm dom}(h)
, and thus
(s,h)\models\lnot\mathtt{alloc}(\mathtt{x})
.

In the second case, (
\mathtt{alloc}(\mathtt{x})\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\varphi^{\prime}
), by definition of
\varphi^{\prime}
we have
\mathtt{alloc}(\mathtt{x})\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\varphi
and
\mathtt{x}\neq\mathtt{x}_{i}\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\varphi
.
By definition of
\textit{\small{Atom}}(\mathtt{x}_{i})
,
s(\mathtt{x})\not\in{\rm dom}(h_{2})
.
By definition of
{\langle\mbox{$\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}$}\rangle}(\varphi,\psi)
,

\lnot\mathtt{alloc}(\mathtt{x})\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}{\langle\mbox{$\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}$}\rangle}(\varphi,\psi)
,
and therefore
s(\mathtt{x})\not\in{\rm dom}(h_{1})
.
Again, by
h=h_{1}{+}h_{2}
,
we have
(s,h)\models\lnot\mathtt{alloc}(\mathtt{x})
.

case:
L\,=\,\lnot\mathtt{x}\hookrightarrow\mathtt{y}
:

Following the definition of
{\langle\mbox{$\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}$}\rangle}(\varphi^{\prime},\psi)
,

\lnot\mathtt{x}\hookrightarrow\mathtt{y}\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\psi
and therefore

\lnot\mathtt{x}\hookrightarrow\mathtt{y}\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}{\langle\mbox{$\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}$}\rangle}(\varphi,\psi)
.
Therefore,
(s,h_{1})\models\lnot\mathtt{x}\hookrightarrow\mathtt{y}
.
Since
{\langle\mbox{$\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}$}\rangle}(\varphi,\psi)
is satisfiable,
\lnot\mathtt{x}\hookrightarrow\mathtt{y}\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\varphi
.
By definition of
\textit{\small{Atom}}(\mathtt{x}_{i})
, we derive
(s,h_{2})\models\lnot\mathtt{x}\hookrightarrow\mathtt{y}
.
From
h=h_{1}{+}h_{2}
,

(s,h)\models\lnot\mathtt{x}\hookrightarrow\mathtt{y}
.

case:
L\,=\,\mathtt{alloc}(\mathtt{x})
:

By definition of
{\langle\mbox{$\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}$}\rangle}(\varphi^{\prime},\psi)
,
we have

\lnot\mathtt{alloc}(\mathtt{x})\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\varphi^{\prime}
and
\mathtt{alloc}(\mathtt{x})\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\psi
.
First, let us suppose
\mathtt{alloc}(\mathtt{x})\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\varphi
.
By definition of
\varphi^{\prime}
,

\mathtt{x}=\mathtt{x}_{i}\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\varphi
and so, by definition of

\textit{\small{Atom}}(\mathtt{x}_{i})
,
s(\mathtt{x})\in{\rm dom}(h_{2})
.
From
h_{2}{\sqsubseteq}h
,
(s,h)\models\mathtt{alloc}(\mathtt{x})
.
Otherwise (
\lnot\mathtt{alloc}(\mathtt{x})\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\varphi
),
by definition of
{\langle\mbox{$\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}$}\rangle}(\varphi,\psi)
,

\mathtt{alloc}(\mathtt{x})\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}{\langle\mbox{$\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}$}\rangle}(\varphi,\psi)
.
So,
s(\mathtt{x})\in{\rm dom}(h_{1})
,
and by
h_{1}{\sqsubseteq}h
,
(s,h)\models\mathtt{alloc}(\mathtt{x})
.

case:
L\,=\,\mathtt{x}\hookrightarrow\mathtt{y}
:

By definition of
{\langle\mbox{$\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}$}\rangle}(\varphi^{\prime},\psi)
, we have

\lnot\mathtt{alloc}(\mathtt{x})\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\varphi^{\prime}
and

{\mathtt{x}\hookrightarrow\mathtt{y}}\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\psi
.
First, suppose
{\mathtt{alloc}(\mathtt{x})\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\varphi}
.
By definition of
\varphi^{\prime}
,

{\mathtt{x}=\mathtt{x}_{i}}\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\varphi
. By definition of

\textit{\small{Atom}}(\mathtt{x}_{i})
,
s(\mathtt{x})\in{\rm dom}(h_{2})
.

Ad absurdum
, suppose
h(s(\mathtt{x}))\neq s(\mathtt{y})
.
By definition of
\textit{\small{Atom}}(\mathtt{x}_{i})
, we have that

{\mathtt{alloc}(\mathtt{x})\land\lnot\mathtt{x}\hookrightarrow\mathtt{y}\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\varphi}
.
However, from
\mathtt{x}\hookrightarrow\mathtt{y}\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\psi
,
this implies
\mathtt{x}\neq\mathtt{x}\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}{\langle\mbox{$\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}$}\rangle}(\varphi,\psi)
, which contradicts the satisfiability of
{\langle\mbox{$\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}$}\rangle}(\varphi,\psi)
.
Therefore,
h(s(\mathtt{x}))=s(\mathtt{y})
and, from
h_{2}{\sqsubseteq}h
, we conclude that
(s,h)\models\mathtt{x}\hookrightarrow\mathtt{y}
.
Otherwise (
\lnot\mathtt{alloc}(\mathtt{x})\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\varphi
),
by definition of
{\langle\mbox{$\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}$}\rangle}(\varphi,\psi)
,

\mathtt{x}\hookrightarrow\mathtt{y}\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}{\langle\mbox{$\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}$}\rangle}(\varphi,\psi)
.
So,
h_{1}(s(\mathtt{x}))=s(\mathtt{y})
,
and by
h_{1}{\sqsubseteq}h
, we derive
(s,h)\models\mathtt{x}\hookrightarrow\mathtt{y}
.

case:
L\,=\,\mathtt{size}\geq\beta_{2}{+}1\mathbin{\text{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raise 4.44444pt\hbox{.}\cr$-$\cr}}}}\beta_{1}
, where
\lnot\mathtt{size}\geq\beta_{1}\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\varphi^{\prime}

and

\mathtt{size}\geq\beta_{2}\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\psi

:

By definition of
\varphi^{\prime}
,

\lnot\mathtt{size}\geq\beta_{1}\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\varphi
, and so
\beta_{1}>\max_{\mathtt{size}}(\varphi)
, since
\varphi
is satisfiable.
By definition of
{\langle\mbox{$\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}$}\rangle}(\varphi,\psi)
and as
\lnot\mathtt{size}\geq\max_{\mathtt{size}}(\varphi)+1\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\varphi
, we have

\mathtt{size}\geq\beta_{2}+1\mathbin{\text{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raise 4.30554pt\hbox{.}\cr$-$\cr}}}}(\max_{\mathtt{size}}(\varphi)+1)\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}{\langle\mbox{$\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}$}\rangle}(\varphi,\psi)
, which in turn implies

{\rm card}({\rm dom}(h_{1}))\geq\beta_{2}\mathbin{\text{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raise 4.30554pt\hbox{.}\cr$-$\cr}}}}\max_{\mathtt{size}}(\varphi)
.
By definition of
\textit{\small{Atom}}(\mathtt{x}_{i})
,
{{\rm card}({\rm dom}(h_{2}))\geq 1}
.
By
{h=h_{1}{+}h_{2}}
,

{\rm card}({\rm dom}(h))\geq(\beta_{2}\mathbin{\text{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raise 4.30554pt\hbox{.}\cr$-$\cr}}}}\max_{\mathtt{size}}(\varphi))+1\geq(\beta_{2}+1)\mathbin{\text{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raise 4.30554pt\hbox{.}\cr$-$\cr}}}}\max_{\mathtt{size}}(\varphi)
.
As
\beta_{1}>\max_{\mathtt{size}}(\varphi)
,

(s,h)\models\mathtt{size}\geq\beta_{2}{+}1\mathbin{\text{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raise 4.30554pt\hbox{.}\cr$-$\cr}}}}\beta_{1}
.

case:
L\,=\,\lnot\mathtt{size}\geq\beta_{2}\mathbin{\text{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raise 4.44444pt\hbox{.}\cr$-$\cr}}}}\beta_{1}
, where
\mathtt{size}\geq\beta_{1}\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\varphi^{\prime}

and
\lnot\mathtt{size}\geq\beta_{2}\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\psi

:

By definition of
\varphi^{\prime}
,
\beta_{1}<\max_{\mathtt{size}}(\varphi)
.
By definition of
{\langle\mbox{$\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}$}\rangle}(\varphi,\psi)
,
we have

\lnot\mathtt{size}\geq\beta_{2}\mathbin{\text{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raise 4.30554pt\hbox{.}\cr$-$\cr}}}}\max_{\mathtt{size}}(\varphi)\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}{\langle\mbox{$\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}$}\rangle}(\varphi,\psi)
.
Notice that, since
{\langle\mbox{$\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}$}\rangle}(\varphi,\psi)
is satisfiable,

\beta_{2}>\max_{\mathtt{size}}(\varphi)
.
Thus,
{\rm card}({\rm dom}(h_{1}))<\beta_{2}-\max_{\mathtt{size}}(\varphi)
.
By definition of
\textit{\small{Atom}}(\mathtt{x}_{i})
,
{\rm card}({\rm dom}(h_{2}))\leq 1
.
From
h=h_{1}{+}h_{2}
,
we conclude that

{\rm card}({\rm dom}(h))<(\beta_{2}-\max_{\mathtt{size}}(\varphi))+1
.
As
\beta_{1}<\max_{\mathtt{size}}(\varphi)
, we have
{\beta_{2}-\max_{\mathtt{size}}(\varphi)+1}\leq\beta_{2}\mathbin{\text{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raise 4.30554pt\hbox{.}\cr$-$\cr}}}}\beta_{1}
.
Therefore,
(s,h)\models\lnot\mathtt{size}\geq\beta_{2}\mathbin{\text{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raise 4.30554pt\hbox{.}\cr$-$\cr}}}}\beta_{1}
.

Continuing the proof of Lemma
13
,
we prove
{\vdash_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathsf{C}}(\ast,\mathbin{-\mkern-6.0mu*})}{\langle\mbox{$\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}$}\rangle}(\varphi,\psi)\Rightarrow(\varphi\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}\top)}
.
Notice that, by the completeness of
\mathcal{H}_{\mathsf{C}}(\ast)
(Theorem
11
),
we conclude that the tautologies in
B. :

and
C. :
are derivable in
\mathcal{H}_{\mathsf{C}}(\ast,\mathbin{-\mkern-6.0mu*})
.
Moreover, notice that
\lnot\mathtt{alloc}(\mathtt{x}_{i})\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\varphi^{\prime}
and, for every
\mathtt{y}\in\mathtt{X}
,
\lnot\mathtt{alloc}(\mathtt{y})\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\varphi
implies
\lnot\mathtt{alloc}(\mathtt{y})\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\varphi^{\prime}
.
This allows us to
rely on the induction hypothesis, and conclude that
\vdash_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathsf{C}}(\ast,\mathbin{-\mkern-6.0mu*})}{\langle\mbox{$\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}$}\rangle}(\varphi^{\prime},\psi)\Rightarrow(\varphi^{\prime}\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}\top)
.
The derivation of
{\langle\mbox{$\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}$}\rangle}(\varphi,\psi)\Rightarrow(\varphi\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}\top)

is given below:

, 5, 6, 7, 8

case:
\max_{\mathtt{size}}(\varphi)=\alpha
:

In this case, we have
\mathtt{size}\geq\alpha\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\varphi
, where we recall that
\alpha=\max_{\mathtt{size}}(\varphi)\geq 1
.
Following the developments of the previous case, we would like to
define a formula
\varphi^{\prime}
for which the formula
\varphi^{\prime}\ast\textit{\small{Atom}}(\mathtt{x}_{i})\Rightarrow\varphi
is valid.
However, since
\varphi
is in
\mathsf{CoreTypes}(\mathtt{X},\alpha)
, we cannot hope for
\varphi^{\prime}
to be a core type in
\mathsf{CoreTypes}(\mathtt{X},\alpha)
.
Indeed, because of
\mathtt{size}\geq\alpha\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\varphi
, in order to achieve the valid formula above we must differentiate between the case where
\varphi
is satisfied by a memory state
(s,h)
such that
{\rm card}({\rm dom}(h))>\alpha
, to the case where
{\rm card}({\rm dom}(h))=\alpha
.
Therefore, below we introduce two core types
\varphi_{\alpha}^{\prime}
and
\varphi_{\alpha-1}^{\prime}
,
and define
\varphi^{\prime}
as
\varphi_{\alpha}^{\prime}\lor\varphi_{\alpha-1}^{\prime}
.
Since the separating conjunction distributes over disjunctions,
after defining these two core types, we can easily adapt the arguments of the previous case to prove that
{\langle\mbox{$\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}$}\rangle}(\varphi,\psi)\Rightarrow(\varphi\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}\top)
.

The formula
\varphi_{\alpha}^{\prime}
is obtained from
\varphi
by replacing,
for every
\mathtt{x}\in\mathtt{X}
such that
\mathtt{x}=\mathtt{x}_{i}\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\varphi
,
every literal
\mathtt{alloc}(\mathtt{x})\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\varphi
with
\lnot\mathtt{alloc}(\mathtt{x})
, and every
\mathtt{x}\hookrightarrow\mathtt{y}\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\varphi
with

\lnot\mathtt{x}\hookrightarrow\mathtt{y}
, where
\mathtt{y}\in\mathtt{X}
.
Notice that
\varphi_{\alpha}^{\prime}
is defined similarly to
\varphi^{\prime}
(in the previous case of the proof), with the exception that we do not modify the polarity of size literals.
Explicitly,
\varphi_{\alpha}^{\prime}
is defined as follows.

\varphi_{\alpha}^{\prime}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mbox{\tiny def}}}{{=}}\begin{aligned} &\bigwedge\{\mathtt{x}\sim\mathtt{y}\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\varphi\mid\sim\in\{=,\neq\}\}\land\bigwedge\{\mathtt{alloc}(\mathtt{x})\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\varphi\mid\mathtt{x}\neq\mathtt{x}_{i}\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\varphi\}\land\bigwedge\{\lnot\mathtt{alloc}(\mathtt{x})\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\varphi\}\land\\
&\bigwedge\{\lnot\mathtt{alloc}(\mathtt{x})\mid\mathtt{x}=\mathtt{x}_{i}\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\varphi\}\land\bigwedge\{\mathtt{x}\hookrightarrow\mathtt{y}\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\varphi\mid\mathtt{x}\neq\mathtt{x}_{i}\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\varphi\}\land\bigwedge\{\lnot\mathtt{x}\hookrightarrow\mathtt{y}\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\varphi\}\land\\
&\bigwedge\{\lnot\mathtt{x}\hookrightarrow\mathtt{y}\mid\mathtt{x}=\mathtt{x}_{i}\land\mathtt{x}\hookrightarrow\mathtt{y}\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\varphi\}\land\bigwedge\{\mathtt{size}\geq\beta\mid\beta\in[0,\alpha-1]\}\land\underline{\mathtt{size}\geq\alpha}.\end{aligned}

The formula
\varphi_{\alpha-1}^{\prime}
is obtained from
\varphi_{\alpha}^{\prime}
by replacing
\mathtt{size}\geq\alpha
(highlighted in the definition of
\varphi_{\alpha}^{\prime}
above), by
\lnot\mathtt{size}\geq\alpha
.
The following properties are satisfied:

D. :

\varphi_{\alpha}^{\prime}
and
\varphi_{\alpha-1}^{\prime}
are satisfiable core types in
\mathsf{CoreTypes}(\mathtt{X},\alpha)
,

E. :

(\textit{\small{Atom}}(\mathtt{x}_{i})\ast(\varphi_{\alpha}^{\prime}\lor\varphi_{\alpha-1}^{\prime}))\Rightarrow\varphi
is valid.

F. :

({\langle\mbox{$\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}$}\rangle}(\varphi,\psi)\ast\textit{\small{Atom}}(\mathtt{x}_{i}))\Rightarrow{\langle\mbox{$\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}$}\rangle}(\varphi_{\alpha}^{\prime},\psi)\lor{\langle\mbox{$\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}$}\rangle}(\varphi_{\alpha-1}^{\prime},\psi)
is valid.

Proof 6.8

(Proof of
D. :
)
.

The proof is very similar to the one of the property
A. :
. Here, we pinpoint the main differences.
First of all,
since both
\varphi_{\alpha}^{\prime}
and
\varphi_{\alpha-1}^{\prime}
are obtained from
\varphi
by changing the polarity of some of the literals in
\mathsf{Lt}(\varphi)
, they are both in
\mathsf{CoreTypes}(\mathtt{X},\alpha)
.
To show that
\varphi_{\alpha}^{\prime}
and
\varphi_{\alpha-1}^{\prime}
are satisfiable,
we rely on the fact that
\varphi
is satisfiable. Let
(s,h)
be a memory state satisfying
\varphi
.
Since
\mathtt{size}\geq\alpha\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\varphi
,
{\rm card}({\rm dom}(h))\geq\alpha
.
Without loss of generality, we can assume
{\rm card}({\rm dom}(h))>\alpha
. Indeed, if

{\rm card}({\rm dom}(h))=\alpha
it is sufficient to add a memory cell
(\ell,\ell)
to
h
, such that
\ell
does not correspond to a program variable
\mathtt{x}\in\mathtt{X}
. It is straightforward to check that the resulting memory state still satisfies
\varphi
.
We introduce a second heap
h^{\prime}
.
Let
\mathtt{L}={\rm dom}(h)\cap\{s(\mathtt{x})\mid\mathtt{x}\in\mathtt{X}\}
be the set of locations in
{\rm dom}(h)
that corresponds to variables in
\mathtt{X}
.
Since
{\rm card}(\mathtt{X})\leq\alpha
,
{\rm card}(\mathtt{L})\leq\alpha
.
Let
h^{\prime}{\sqsubseteq}h
such that
\mathtt{L}\subseteq{\rm dom}(h^{\prime})

and
{\rm card}({\rm dom}(h^{\prime}))=\alpha
.
Again, it is straightforward to see that
(s,h^{\prime})
satisfies
\varphi
.
Intuitively, we rely on
(s,h)
to show that
\varphi_{\alpha}^{\prime}
is satisfiable, and on
(s,h^{\prime})
to show that
\varphi_{\alpha-1}^{\prime}
is satisfiable.
As
\mathtt{alloc}(\mathtt{x}_{i})\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\varphi
, we have
s(\mathtt{x})\in{\rm dom}(h)
and
s(\mathtt{x})\in{\rm dom}(h^{\prime})
.
We consider heaps
h_{1}
and
h_{2}
such that
h=h_{1}{+}h_{2}
and
{\rm dom}(h_{1})=\{s(\mathtt{x}_{i})\}
.
Similarly, we consider heaps
h_{1}^{\prime}
and
h_{2}^{\prime}
such that
h^{\prime}=h_{1}^{\prime}{+}h_{2}^{\prime}
and
{\rm dom}(h_{1}^{\prime})=\{s(\mathtt{x}_{i})\}
.
We show that
(s,h_{2})\models\varphi_{\alpha}^{\prime}
and

(s,h_{2}^{\prime})\models\varphi_{\alpha-1}^{\prime}
.
Let us first discuss the former result. Let

L\in\mathsf{Lt}(\varphi_{\alpha}^{\prime})
.
If
L
is not of the form
\mathtt{size}\geq\beta
or
\lnot\mathtt{size}\geq\beta
,
then
(s,h_{2})\models L
follows exactly as in the proof
of
A. :
. Otherwise,

case:
L\,=\,\mathtt{size}\geq\beta
:

By definition of
h_{2}
,
{\rm card}({\rm dom}(h_{2}))={\rm card}({\rm dom}(h))-1\geq\alpha
. Since
\beta\leq\alpha
(as
\varphi_{\alpha}^{\prime}
is in
\mathsf{CoreTypes}(\mathtt{X},\alpha)
), we conclude that
(s,h_{2})\models\mathtt{size}\geq\beta
.

case:
L\,=\,\lnot\mathtt{size}\geq\beta
:

By definition of
\varphi_{\alpha}^{\prime}
, no literals of the form
{\lnot\mathtt{size}\geq\beta}
belongs to
\mathsf{Lt}(\varphi_{\alpha}^{\prime})
. Therefore,
this case does not occur.

This concludes the proof of
(s,h_{2})\models\varphi_{\alpha}^{\prime}
.
For the proof of
(s,h_{2}^{\prime})\models\varphi_{\alpha-1}^{\prime}
,
let us consider
L\in\mathsf{Lt}(\varphi_{\alpha-1}^{\prime})
.
Again, if
L
is not of the form
\mathtt{size}\geq\beta
or
\lnot\mathtt{size}\geq\alpha
,
then
(s,h_{2}^{\prime})\models L
follows exactly as in the proof
of
A. :
(replacing
h
by
h^{\prime}
and
h_{2}
by
h_{2}^{\prime}
). Otherwise,

case:
L\,=\,\mathtt{size}\geq\beta
:

By definition of
\varphi_{\alpha-1}^{\prime}
, we have
\beta<\alpha
.
By definition of
h_{2}^{\prime}
,
{\rm card}({\rm dom}(h_{2}^{\prime}))={\rm card}({\rm dom}(h^{\prime}))-1=\alpha-1
. Therefore,
(s,h_{2}^{\prime})\models\mathtt{size}\geq\beta
.

case:
L\,=\,\lnot\mathtt{size}\geq\beta
:

By definition of
\varphi_{\alpha-1}^{\prime}
,
\beta=\alpha
.
Since
{\rm card}({\rm dom}(h_{2}^{\prime}))=\alpha-1
,
we conclude that
(s,h_{2}^{\prime})\models\lnot\mathtt{size}\geq\beta
.

Proof 6.9

(Proof of
E. :
)
.

The proof is very similar to the one of the
property
B. :
.
We show that
(\textit{\small{Atom}}(\mathtt{x}_{i})\ast\varphi_{\alpha}^{\prime})\Rightarrow\varphi
and

(\textit{\small{Atom}}(\mathtt{x}_{i})\ast\varphi_{\alpha-1}^{\prime})\Rightarrow\varphi
.
Then,
E. :
follows as the separating conjunction distributes over disjunction.
First, let us consider
(\textit{\small{Atom}}(\mathtt{x}_{i})\ast\varphi_{\alpha}^{\prime})\Rightarrow\varphi
, and a memory state
(s,h)
satisfying
\textit{\small{Atom}}(\mathtt{x}_{i})\ast\varphi_{\alpha}^{\prime}
.
There are
h_{1}
and
h_{2}
such that
h=h_{1}{+}h_{2}
,
(s,h_{1})\models\textit{\small{Atom}}(\mathtt{x}_{i})
and
(s,h_{2})\models\varphi_{\alpha}^{\prime}
.
Let
L\in\mathsf{Lt}(\varphi)
.
Notice that
\varphi
does not contain negated
\mathtt{size}\geq\beta
literals.
If
L
is not
\mathtt{size}\geq\beta
, for some
\beta\in[0,\alpha]
,
then
(s,h)\models L
follows exactly as it is shown in the proof of
B. :
.
Otherwise, suppose
L=\mathtt{size}\geq\beta
, where
\beta\in[0,\alpha]
. By definition
of
\varphi_{\alpha}^{\prime}
,

\mathtt{size}\geq\alpha\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\varphi_{\alpha}^{\prime}
.
Hence,
{\rm card}({\rm dom}(h_{2}))\geq\alpha
and, from
h_{2}{\sqsubseteq}h
,
we derive
(s,h)\models\mathtt{size}\geq\beta
.
So,
(s,h)\models\varphi
.

Let us now consider
(\textit{\small{Atom}}(\mathtt{x}_{i})\ast\varphi_{\alpha-1}^{\prime})\Rightarrow\varphi
and a memory state
(s,h)
satisfying
\textit{\small{Atom}}(\mathtt{x}_{i})\ast\varphi_{\alpha-1}^{\prime}
.
There are
h_{1}
and
h_{2}
such that
h=h_{1}{+}h_{2}
,
(s,h_{1})\models\textit{\small{Atom}}(\mathtt{x}_{i})
and
(s,h_{2})\models\varphi_{\alpha-1}^{\prime}
.
Let
L\in\mathsf{Lt}(\varphi)
.
Again,
\varphi
does not contain negated
\mathtt{size}\geq\beta
literals, and if

L
is not
\mathtt{size}\geq\beta
, for some
\beta\in[0,\alpha]
,
then
(s,h)\models L
follows exactly as is shown in the proof of
B. :
.
Otherwise, suppose
L=\mathtt{size}\geq\beta
, where
\beta\in[0,\alpha]
.
By definition of
\varphi_{\alpha-1}^{\prime}
,

\mathtt{size}\geq\alpha\mathbin{\text{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raise 4.30554pt\hbox{.}\cr$-$\cr}}}}1\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\varphi_{\alpha-1}^{\prime}
.
Therefore,
{\rm card}({\rm dom}(h_{2}))\geq\alpha-1
.
By definition of
\textit{\small{Atom}}(\mathtt{x}_{i})
,
{\rm card}({\rm dom}(h_{1}))=1
.
From
h=h_{1}{+}h_{2}
,
we conclude that
{\rm card}({\rm dom}(h))\geq\alpha
and thus
(s,h)\models\mathtt{size}\geq\beta
.
Therefore,
(s,h)\models\varphi
.

Proof 6.10

(Proof of
F. :
)
.

Recall that
{\langle\mbox{$\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}$}\rangle}(\varphi,\psi)
is satisfiable.
In particular, from its definition together with
\mathtt{size}\geq\alpha\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\varphi
, this implies that
{\mathtt{size}\geq\alpha}\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\psi
, as otherwise we would have
\lnot\mathtt{size}\geq 0\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}{\langle\mbox{$\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}$}\rangle}(\varphi,\psi)
. So, as
\psi
is a satisfiable core type in
\mathsf{CoreTypes}(\mathtt{X},\alpha)
,
for all
\beta\in[0,\alpha]
,

\mathtt{size}\geq\beta\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\psi
.
Alternatively,
\psi
does not contain
\lnot\mathtt{size}\geq\beta
literals.
We look at the definitions of
{\langle\mbox{$\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}$}\rangle}(\varphi_{\alpha}^{\prime},\psi)

and
{\langle\mbox{$\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}$}\rangle}(\varphi_{\alpha-1}^{\prime},\psi)
.

a. :

Since for all

\beta\in[0,\alpha]
,

\mathtt{size}\geq\beta\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\varphi_{\alpha}^{\prime}
and
\mathtt{size}\geq\beta\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\psi
,
we derive that
{\langle\mbox{$\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}$}\rangle}(\varphi_{\alpha}^{\prime},\psi)

does not contain
\mathtt{size}\geq\beta
nor
\lnot\mathtt{size}\geq\beta
literals (for all
{\beta\in[0,\alpha]}
).
This holds directly by definition of

{\langle\mbox{$\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}$}\rangle}(\varphi_{\alpha}^{\prime},\psi)
, which can be retrieved by substituting
\varphi^{\prime}
by
\varphi_{\alpha}^{\prime}
in Figure
9
.

b. :

Analogously, we know that

\lnot\mathtt{size}\geq\alpha\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\varphi_{\alpha-1}^{\prime}
whereas for every
\beta\in[0,\alpha-1]
,
\mathtt{size}\geq\beta\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\varphi_{\alpha-1}^{\prime}
,
and therefore among all the literals
\mathtt{size}\geq\beta
or

\lnot\mathtt{size}\geq\beta
(
\beta\in[0,\alpha]
),

{\langle\mbox{$\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}$}\rangle}(\varphi_{\alpha-1}^{\prime},\psi)

only contains
\mathtt{size}\geq 1
(occurring positively).

By definition and with the sole exception of the polarity of the formula
{\mathtt{size}\geq\alpha}
(occurring positively in
\varphi_{\alpha}^{\prime}
and negatively in
\varphi_{\alpha-1}^{\prime}
), the two core types

\varphi_{\alpha-1}^{\prime}
and
\varphi_{\alpha}^{\prime}
are equal.
Directly by definition of

{\langle\mbox{$\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}$}\rangle}(\varphi_{\alpha}^{\prime},\psi)

and

{\langle\mbox{$\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}$}\rangle}(\varphi_{\alpha-1}^{\prime},\psi)
,
together with
a. :
and
b. :
,
this implies that
{\langle\mbox{$\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}$}\rangle}(\varphi_{\alpha-1}^{\prime},\psi)

is syntactically equal to
{{\langle\mbox{$\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}$}\rangle}(\varphi_{\alpha}^{\prime},\psi)\land\mathtt{size}\geq 1}
(up to commutativity and associativity of conjunction).
This means that the formula
{\langle\mbox{$\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}$}\rangle}(\varphi_{\alpha-1}^{\prime},\psi)\Rightarrow{\langle\mbox{$\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}$}\rangle}(\varphi_{\alpha}^{\prime},\psi)
is valid,
and suggests us that,
in order to show
F. :
,
we can simply establish that
({\langle\mbox{$\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}$}\rangle}(\varphi,\psi)\ast\textit{\small{Atom}}(\mathtt{x}_{i}))\Rightarrow{\langle\mbox{$\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}$}\rangle}(\varphi_{\alpha}^{\prime},\psi)
is valid.
As we already stated,
\varphi_{\alpha}^{\prime}
is defined as
\varphi^{\prime}
(in the previous step of the proof), with the exception that we do not modify the polarity of
\mathtt{size}\geq\beta
literals.
Because of this, we can rely on the proof of
C. :
.
Briefly,
we consider a memory state
(s,h)
satisfying
{\langle\mbox{$\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}$}\rangle}(\varphi,\psi)\ast\textit{\small{Atom}}(\mathtt{x}_{i})
.
There are
h_{1}
and
h_{2}
such that
h=h_{1}{+}h_{2}
,

(s,h_{1})\models{\langle\mbox{$\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}$}\rangle}(\varphi,\psi)
and

(s,h_{2})\models\textit{\small{Atom}}(\mathtt{x}_{i})
.
Let
L\in\mathsf{Lt}({\langle\mbox{$\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}$}\rangle}(\varphi_{\alpha-1}^{\prime},\psi))
.
By
a. :
,
L
is neither of the form
\mathtt{size}\geq\beta
nor of the form
\lnot\mathtt{size}\geq\beta
.
Therefore,
(s,h)\models L
follows exactly as shown in the proof of
C. :
.

We are now ready to prove that
{\langle\mbox{$\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}$}\rangle}(\varphi,\psi)\Rightarrow(\varphi\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}\top)
.
By Theorem
11
, the tautologies
in
D. :
and
F. :

are derivable in
\mathcal{H}_{\mathsf{C}}(\ast,\mathbin{-\mkern-6.0mu*})
. Moreover,
since
\lnot\mathtt{alloc}(\mathtt{x}_{i})\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\{\varphi_{\alpha}^{\prime}\mathbin{;}\varphi_{\alpha-1}^{\prime}\}
and, for every
\mathtt{y}\in\mathtt{X}
,
\lnot\mathtt{alloc}(\mathtt{y})\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\varphi
implies
\lnot\mathtt{alloc}(\mathtt{y})\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\{\varphi_{\alpha}^{\prime}\mathbin{;}\varphi_{\alpha-1}^{\prime}\}
,
we rely on the induction hypothesis to derive

\vdash_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathsf{C}}(\ast,\mathbin{-\mkern-6.0mu*})}{\langle\mbox{$\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}$}\rangle}(\varphi_{\alpha}^{\prime},\psi)\Rightarrow(\varphi_{\alpha}^{\prime}\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}\top),\qquad\vdash_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathsf{C}}(\ast,\mathbin{-\mkern-6.0mu*})}{\langle\mbox{$\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}$}\rangle}(\varphi_{\alpha-1}^{\prime},\psi)\Rightarrow(\varphi_{\alpha-1}^{\prime}\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}\top).

We derive
{\langle\mbox{$\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}$}\rangle}(\varphi,\psi)\Rightarrow(\varphi\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}\top)
(see Figure
43
) concluding the proof of Lemma
13

.

Lemma
13
in which
\varphi
and
\psi

are core types can be extended to arbitrary Boolean combinations of core formulae, as we show that the distributivity of
\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}
over disjunctions is provable in
\mathcal{H}_{\mathsf{C}}(\ast,\mathbin{-\mkern-6.0mu*})
.
As a consequence of this development, we achieve the main result of the paper.

Theorem 16
.

\mathcal{H}_{\mathsf{C}}(\ast,\mathbin{-\mkern-6.0mu*})
is sound and complete for
\mathsf{SL(}\ast,\mathbin{-\mkern-6.0mu*}\mathsf{)}
.

Proof 6.11
.

Soundness of the proof system
\mathcal{H}_{\mathsf{C}}(\ast,\mathbin{-\mkern-6.0mu*})
has been already established earlier, see Lemma
1
.
As far as the completeness proof is concerned, its structure is very similar to the proof
of Theorem
11
except that we have to be able to handle the separating
implication. In order to be self-contained, we reproduce some of its arguments albeit adapted to

\mathcal{H}_{\mathsf{C}}(\ast,\mathbin{-\mkern-6.0mu*})
.

We need to show that for every formula
\varphi

in
\mathsf{SL(}\ast,\mathbin{-\mkern-6.0mu*}\mathsf{)}
, there is a Boolean combination of core formulae
\psi
such that

\vdash_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathsf{C}}(\ast,\mathbin{-\mkern-6.0mu*})}\varphi\Leftrightarrow\psi
. In order to conclude the proof,
when
\varphi
is valid for
\mathsf{SL(}\ast,\mathbin{-\mkern-6.0mu*}\mathsf{)}
, by soundness of
\mathcal{H}_{\mathsf{C}}(\ast,\mathbin{-\mkern-6.0mu*})
, we obtain that

\psi
is valid too and therefore
\vdash_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathsf{C}}(\ast,\mathbin{-\mkern-6.0mu*})}\psi

as
\mathcal{H}_{\mathsf{C}}
is a subsystem of
\mathcal{H}_{\mathsf{C}}(\ast,\mathbin{-\mkern-6.0mu*})
and
\mathcal{H}_{\mathsf{C}}
is complete by
Theorem
5
. By propositional reasoning, we get that

\vdash_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathsf{C}}(\ast,\mathbin{-\mkern-6.0mu*})}\varphi
.

In order to show that every formula
\varphi
has a provably equivalent Boolean combination
of core formulae, we heavily rely on Corollary
10
and on Lemma
13
. The proof is by simple induction
on the number of occurrences of
\ast
or
\mathbin{-\mkern-6.0mu*}
in
\varphi
that are not involved in the definition of some core
formula of the form
\mathtt{size}\geq\beta
or
\mathtt{alloc}(\mathtt{x})
. For the base case, when
\varphi

has no occurrence of the separating connectives,
\mathtt{x}=\mathtt{y}
and
\mathtt{x}\hookrightarrow\mathtt{y}
are already core formulae, whereas
\mathtt{emp}
is logically equivalent to
\neg\mathtt{size}\geq 1
.

Before performing the induction step, let us observe that in
\mathcal{H}_{\mathsf{C}}(\ast,\mathbin{-\mkern-6.0mu*})
, the replacement of provably equivalent
formulae holds true, which is stated as follows:

R1

Let
\varphi,\varphi^{\prime}
and
\psi
be
formulae of
\mathsf{SL(}\ast,\mathbin{-\mkern-6.0mu*}\mathsf{)}
such that

\vdash_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathsf{C}}(\ast,\mathbin{-\mkern-6.0mu*})}\varphi\Leftrightarrow\varphi^{\prime}
. Then,

\vdash_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathsf{C}}(\ast,\mathbin{-\mkern-6.0mu*})}\psi[\varphi]_{\rho}\Rightarrow\psi[\varphi^{\prime}]_{\rho}

In order to prove
R1
, we are almost done as we have already shown
R0

in the proof of Theorem
11
and the same properties hold for
\mathsf{SL(}\ast,\mathbin{-\mkern-6.0mu*}\mathsf{)}
though
the language is richer.

As a direct consequence of the admissibility of the rules
(

I

-
⁣
∗

⁢

.7

)
and
(

I

-
⁣
∗

⁢

.9

)

from Lemma
14
,
the rules below are also admissible:

\text{}\frac{\displaystyle\varphi\Leftrightarrow\varphi^{\prime}}{\displaystyle\varphi\mathbin{-\mkern-6.0mu*}\psi\Leftrightarrow\varphi^{\prime}\mathbin{-\mkern-6.0mu*}\psi}\ \ \ \ \ \text{}\frac{\displaystyle\varphi\Leftrightarrow\varphi^{\prime}}{\displaystyle\psi\mathbin{-\mkern-6.0mu*}\varphi\Leftrightarrow\psi\mathbin{-\mkern-6.0mu*}\varphi^{\prime}}

We need the two rules as
\mathbin{-\mkern-6.0mu*}
is not commutative.
Consequently, by structural induction on
\psi
, one can conclude that

\vdash_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathsf{C}}(\ast,\mathbin{-\mkern-6.0mu*})}\varphi\Leftrightarrow\varphi^{\prime}

implies
\vdash_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathsf{C}}(\ast,\mathbin{-\mkern-6.0mu*})}\psi[\varphi]_{\rho}\Rightarrow\psi[\varphi^{\prime}]_{\rho}
.

Now, assume
\varphi
is a formula in
\mathsf{SL(}\ast,\mathbin{-\mkern-6.0mu*}\mathsf{)}
. Without loss of generality, we can assume that
the separating connectives in
\varphi
are restricted to
\ast
and
\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}

for the occurrences that are not related to abbreviations for core formulae. Indeed,

\psi^{\prime}\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}\psi
is a shortcut for
\neg(\psi^{\prime}\mathbin{-\mkern-6.0mu*}\neg\psi)

and therefore one can replace every occurrence of
\psi^{\prime}\mathbin{-\mkern-6.0mu*}\psi

by
\neg(\psi^{\prime}\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}\neg\psi)
assuming that
\psi^{\prime}
and
\psi
are already of the appropriate shape.
Such a replacement is possible thanks to
R1
.

Assume that
\varphi
is a formula in
\mathsf{SL(}\ast,\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}\mathsf{)}
with
n+1
occurrences of
\ast

or
\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}
not involved in the definition of core formulae.

Let
\psi
be a subformula of
\varphi
(at the occurrence
\rho
) of the form
\psi_{1}\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}\psi_{2}
such that

\psi_{1}
and
\psi_{2}
are in
\mathsf{Bool}(\mathsf{Core}(\mathtt{X},\alpha_{1}))
and

\mathsf{Bool}(\mathsf{Core}(\mathtt{X},\alpha_{2}))
, respectively.
By propositional reasoning, one can show that there are formulae in disjunctive normal form

\psi_{1}^{1}\vee\cdots\vee\psi_{1}^{n_{1}}
and
\psi_{2}^{1}\vee\cdots\vee\psi_{2}^{n_{2}}

such that
\vdash_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathsf{C}}}\psi_{i}\Leftrightarrow\psi_{i}^{1}\vee\cdots\vee\psi_{i}^{n_{i}}

for
i\in\{1,2\}
, and moreover every
\psi_{i}^{j}
’s is a core type in
\mathsf{CoreTypes}(\mathtt{X},\max({\rm card}(\mathtt{X}),\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}))
.
Again, by using propositional reasoning but this time establishing also distributivity of
\vee
over
\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}
, we have

\vdash_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathsf{C}}(\ast,\mathbin{-\mkern-6.0mu*})}\psi_{1}\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}\psi_{2}\Leftrightarrow\bigvee_{j_{1}\in[1,n_{1}],j_{2}\in[1,n_{2}]}\psi_{1}^{j_{1}}\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}\psi_{2}^{j_{2}}.

We rely on Lemma
13
, and conclude that there is
a conjunction of core formulae
\psi^{j_{1},j_{2}}
in
\mathsf{Conj}(\mathsf{Core}(\mathtt{X},\max({\rm card}(\mathtt{X}),\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2})))

such that
\vdash_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathsf{C}}(\ast,\mathbin{-\mkern-6.0mu*})}\psi_{1}^{j_{1}}\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}\psi_{2}^{j_{2}}\Leftrightarrow\psi^{j_{1},j_{2}}
.
By propositional reasoning, we get

\vdash_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathsf{C}}(\ast,\mathbin{-\mkern-6.0mu*})}\psi_{1}\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}\psi_{2}\Leftrightarrow\bigvee_{j_{1}\in[1,n_{1}],j_{2}\in[1,n_{2}]}\psi^{j_{1},j_{2}}.

Consequently (thanks to the property
R1
), we obtain

\vdash_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathsf{C}}(\ast,\mathbin{-\mkern-6.0mu*})}\varphi\Leftrightarrow\varphi[\bigvee_{j_{1}\in[1,n_{1}],j_{2}\in[1,n_{2}]}\psi^{j_{1},j_{2}}]_{\rho}

Note that the right-hand side formula has
n
occurrences of the separating
connnectives that are not involved in the definition of some core
formula. The induction hypothesis applies, which concludes the proof.

The case when
\psi
is a subformula of
\varphi
(at the occurrence
\rho
)
of the form
\psi_{1}\ast\psi_{2}
is treated as in the proof of Theorem
11

and therefore is omitted herein.

7.
Related work

In this section, we briefly compare our Hilbert-style proof system
\mathcal{H}_{\mathsf{C}}(\ast,\mathbin{-\mkern-6.0mu*})

with existing proof systems for
\mathsf{SL(}\ast,\mathbin{-\mkern-6.0mu*}\mathsf{)}
, fragments or extensions and we recall a few landmark works
proposing proof systems for abstract separation logics or for logics that are variants of Boolean BI.
Those
latter proof systems are not necessarily Hilbert-style and may contain labels or other similar machineries.
So, this section completes the presentation of the context from Section
1

while pinpointing the main original features of our calculus.
Finally, we also evoke several works that use the idea of axiomatising a fragment of a logic
and to provide in the proof system means to transform any formula into an equivalent formula from that
fragment. This is clearly similar to the approach we have followed, but we aim at picking examples from
outside the realm of spatial and resource logics. In order to keep the length of this section reasonable,
we limit ourselves to the main bibliographical entries but additional relevant works can be found in the cited materials.

Proof systems for quantifier-free separation logic.

Surprisingly, as far as we know, sound and complete proof systems for
\mathsf{SL(}\ast,\mathbin{-\mkern-6.0mu*}\mathsf{)}
are very rare
and the only system we are aware of is a tableaux-based calculus from
[
GM10
]

with labelled formulae (each formula is enriched with a label to be interpreted by
some heap) and with resource graphs to encode symbolically constraints between heap expressions (i.e. labels). Of course,
translations from separation logics into
logics or theories have been designed, see e.g.
[
CGH05
,
RISK16
]
,
but the finding of proof systems for
\mathsf{SL(}\ast,\mathbin{-\mkern-6.0mu*}\mathsf{)}
with all Boolean connectives and the separating
connectives
\ast
and
\mathbin{-\mkern-6.0mu*}
has been quite challenging.
Unlike
[
GM10
]
,
\mathcal{H}_{\mathsf{C}}(\ast,\mathbin{-\mkern-6.0mu*})
uses only
\mathsf{SL(}\ast,\mathbin{-\mkern-6.0mu*}\mathsf{)}
formulae and therefore
can be viewed as a quite orthodox Hilbert-style calculus with no extra syntactic objects.
In particular,
\mathcal{H}_{\mathsf{C}}(\ast,\mathbin{-\mkern-6.0mu*})
has no syntactic machinery to refer to heaps or to other
semantical objects related to
\mathsf{SL(}\ast,\mathbin{-\mkern-6.0mu*}\mathsf{)}
.
In
[
GM10
]
, the resource graphs attached to the tableaux are designed
to reason about heap constraints, and to provide control for designing strategies that lead
to termination. Interestingly, the calculus in
[
GM10
]
is
intended to be helpful
to synthesize countermodels (which is a standard feature for labelled deduction systems
[
Gab96
]
)
or to be extended to the first-order case, which is partly done
in
[
GM10
]
but we know that completeness is theoretically impossible.
Besides, a sound labelled sequent calculus for the first-order extension of
\mathsf{SL(}\ast,\mathbin{-\mkern-6.0mu*}\mathsf{)}
is presented in
[
HGT15
]
but completeness for the sublogic
\mathsf{SL(}\ast,\mathbin{-\mkern-6.0mu*}\mathsf{)}
is not established.
The calculus in
[
HGT15
]
has also labels, which differs from our puristic approach.
A complete sequent-style calculus for the symbolic heap fragment has been designed quite early
in
[
BCO04
]
but does not deal with full
\mathsf{SL(}\ast,\mathbin{-\mkern-6.0mu*}\mathsf{)}
(in particular it is
not closed under Boolean connectives and does not contain the separating implication).
A complexity-wise optimal decision procedure for the symbolic heap fragment is designed in
[
CHO+11
]

based on a characterisation in terms of homomorphisms.

Frameworks for abstract separation logics.

Bunched logics, such as the bunched logic BI introduced in
[
OP99
]
,
are known to be closely related to separation logics that can be
viewed as concretisation of (Boolean) BI with models made of memory states,
see e.g.
[
Pym02
,
Rey02
,
GM05
,
PSO18
]
.
Actually, bunched logics come with different flavours, Boolean BI being considered as the genuine abstract
version of
\mathsf{SL(}\ast,\mathbin{-\mkern-6.0mu*}\mathsf{)}
. Though Boolean BI has been shown undecidable in
[
LG13
,
BK14
]
,
a Hilbert-style axiomatisation can be found in
[
GLW06
]
. Our proof system
\mathcal{H}_{\mathsf{C}}(\ast,\mathbin{-\mkern-6.0mu*})
inherits
all the axiom schemas and inference rules for Boolean BI from
[
GLW06
]
, which is expected
as

\mathsf{SL(}\ast,\mathbin{-\mkern-6.0mu*}\mathsf{)}
can be viewed as Boolean BI on concrete heaps but with the notable difference of having built-in atomic formulae

\mathtt{x}=\mathtt{y}

and
\mathtt{x}\hookrightarrow\mathtt{y}
.
Bunched logics, such as Boolean BI, can be defined in several ways, for instance assuming classical or intuitionistic
connectives, and in
[
Bro12
]
, a unified proof theory based on display calculi
[
Bel82
]

is designed for a variety of four bunched logics, including Boolean BI (see also the nested
sequent calculus for Boolean BI in
[
PSP13
]
). In display calculi, structural connectives
enrich the sequent-style structures, providing a family of structural connectives accompanying the standard
comma from sequent-style calculi. The main results in
[
Bro12
]
include cut-elimination, soundness
and completeness. So, compared to our calculus
\mathcal{H}_{\mathsf{C}}(\ast,\mathbin{-\mkern-6.0mu*})
, the calculi in
[
Bro12
]
are designed
for logics with more abstract semantical structures and owns a proof-theoretical machinery that does not
include labels but instead complex structured sequents.

The quest for designing frameworks dedicated to classes of abstract separation logics have been pursued in several directions.
For instance, models for Boolean BI are typically relational commutative monoids but properties can be
added leading to a separation theory. In
[
BV14
]
,
a hybrid version of Boolean BI is introduced, called HyBBI, in which nominals (in the sense of hybrid modal logics, see
e.g.
[
ABM01
]
) are added in order to be able to express rich standard properties in
separation theory, such as cancellativity. Not only an Hilbert-style proof system is provided for HyBBI
[
BV14
]

but also a parametric completeness result is shown. More precisely, any extension of the proof system for HyBBI
with a set of specific axioms is actually complete with respect to the class of models that satisfy the axioms,
which is analogous to Sahlqvist’s Theorem for modal logics
[
Sah75
,
BdRV01
]
.
This provides a very general means to axiomatise variants of Boolean BI but at the cost of having the extra machinery
for nominals. Moreover, as HyBBI and its extensions are abstract separation logics with no atomic formulae of
the form
\mathtt{x}=\mathtt{y}
or
\mathtt{x}\hookrightarrow\mathtt{y}
, the tools developed in
[
BV14
]

are of no help to design an Hilbert-style proof system for
\mathsf{SL(}\ast,\mathbin{-\mkern-6.0mu*}\mathsf{)}
(except that its part dealing with Boolean BI
is precisely borrowed from
[
GLW06
]
too).

Besides, in
[
HCGT18
]
labelled sequent calculi are designed for several abstract separation logics by considering different sets of
properties. The sequents contain labelled formulae (a formula prefixed by a label to be interpreted
as an abstract heap) as well as relational atoms to express relationships between abstract heaps. Though the framework in
[
HCGT18
]

is modular and very general to handle abstract separation logics, it is not tailored to separation logics with concrete semantics,
see
[
HCGT18
, Section 7]
for possible future directions. In contrast, as explained already, the paper
[
HGT15
]
deals with first-order
separation logic with concrete semantics and presents a sound labelled sequent calculus for it. Of course, the calculus
cannot be complete but more importantly in the context of the current paper, completeness is not established for the quantifier-free fragment.
In
[
HGT15
]
, the sequents contain labelled formulae and relational atoms, similarly to
[
HCGT18
]
(see also
[
Hóu15
]
).
Hence, this does not meet our requirements to have a pure axiomatisation in which only logical formulae
from quantifier-free separation logic are allowed.

Modularity of the approaches from
[
Bro12
,
BV14
,
HCGT18
]
is further developed in the recent
work
[
DP18
,
Doc19
]
by proposing a framework for labelled tableaux systems parametrised by the choice of separation theories
(in the very sense of
[
BV14
]
). It is remarkable that the developments in
[
DP18
,
Doc19
]

are very general as it can handle separation theories that can be expressed in the rich class of so-called coherent first-order
formulae, included in the first-order fragment
\Pi_{2}
. The first-order axioms are directly translated into inference rules.
The calculi use labelled formulae (every formula is decorated by a sign and by a label) as well as constraints enforcing
properties between worlds/resources. Unlike
[
GM10
]
, the reasoning about labels is not outsourced
but handled directly by the calculus. As several works mentioned above, the framework in
[
DP18
,
Doc19
]
does not
provide for free a proof system for
\mathsf{SL(}\ast,\mathbin{-\mkern-6.0mu*}\mathsf{)}
(which might have been a close cousin of the one in
[
GM10
]
).
More importantly, similarly to the works
[
GM10
,
BV14
,
HCGT18
]
, the labelled tableaux systems handle
syntactic objects referring to semantical concepts related to the abstract separation logics that go beyond the only presence
of formulae. In a way, modularity of the approach prevents from having a puristic calculus for
\mathsf{SL(}\ast,\mathbin{-\mkern-6.0mu*}\mathsf{)}
, apart from the fact that

\mathsf{SL(}\ast,\mathbin{-\mkern-6.0mu*}\mathsf{)}
is not part of the logics handled in
[
DP18
]
.

Axiomatising knowledge logics with reduction axioms.

In order to conclude this section, let us recall that the derivations in

\mathcal{H}_{\mathsf{C}}(\ast,\mathbin{-\mkern-6.0mu*})
are able to simulate the bottom-up elimination of
separating connectives, leading to Boolean combinations of core formulae
for which the system
\mathcal{H}_{\mathsf{C}}(\ast,\mathbin{-\mkern-6.0mu*})
is also complete. As the core formulae
are (simple) formulae in
\mathsf{SL(}\ast,\mathbin{-\mkern-6.0mu*}\mathsf{)}
, the axiomatisation provided by
\mathcal{H}_{\mathsf{C}}(\ast,\mathbin{-\mkern-6.0mu*})

uses only
\mathsf{SL(}\ast,\mathbin{-\mkern-6.0mu*}\mathsf{)}
formulae and is complete for the full logic
\mathsf{SL(}\ast,\mathbin{-\mkern-6.0mu*}\mathsf{)}
(and not only
for Boolean combinations of core formulae).
Note that as a by-product of our completeness proof for
\mathsf{SL(}\ast,\mathbin{-\mkern-6.0mu*}\mathsf{)}
, we get expressive
completeness of
\mathsf{SL(}\ast,\mathbin{-\mkern-6.0mu*}\mathsf{)}
with respect to Boolean combinations of core formulae,
with a proof different from the developments in
[
Loz04a
,
BDL09
,
EIP19
]
.

This general principle described above is familiar for axiomatising dynamic epistemic
logics in which dynamic connectives might be eliminated with the help of so-called

reduction axioms
, see e.g. standard examples
in
[
vDvdHK08
,
vB11
,
WC13
,
FVQ19
]
.
In a nutshell, every formula containing a dynamic operator is provably reduced to a formula without such an operator.
Completeness is then established thanks to the completeness of the underlying ‘basic’ language,
A similar approach for the linear
\mu
-calculus is recently presented in
[
Dou17
]

for which a form of constructive completeness is advocated, see also
[
Lüc18
]
.
Hilbert-style axiomatisations following similar high-level principles for the
modal separation logics MSL(
\ast
,
\Diamond
) and MSL(
\ast
,
\langle\neq\rangle
)
introduced in
[
DF19
]
, have been designed in
[
DFM19
]
.

8.
Conclusion

We presented a method to axiomatise internally quantifier-free separation logic
\mathsf{SL(}\ast,\mathbin{-\mkern-6.0mu*}\mathsf{)}
based on the axiomatisation of Boolean combinations of core formulae
(and even more precisely, based on the restricted fragment of core types).
We designed the first proof system for
\mathsf{SL(}\ast,\mathbin{-\mkern-6.0mu*}\mathsf{)}
that is completely internal and highlights the essential ingredients
of the heaplet semantics.
The fact that the calculus is internal simply means that the
axioms and inference rules
involve schemas instantiated by formulae in
\mathsf{SL(}\ast,\mathbin{-\mkern-6.0mu*}\mathsf{)}
(no use of nominals, labels or other
syntactic objects that are not
\mathsf{SL(}\ast,\mathbin{-\mkern-6.0mu*}\mathsf{)}
formulae).
Obviously, the Hilbert-style proof system presented in the paper
is of theoretical interest, at least to grasp what are the essential features of
\mathsf{SL(}\ast,\mathbin{-\mkern-6.0mu*}\mathsf{)}
. Still, it remains
to be seen whether applications are possible for designing decision procedures, for instance
to feed provers with appropriate axiom instances to accelerate the proof search.
Furthermore, we have not investigated whether the proof system
\mathcal{H}_{\mathsf{C}}(\ast,\mathbin{-\mkern-6.0mu*})
(see Figure
1
)
can be simplified without loosing completeness. This might be rewarding for using
the calculus for other logics or for other applications. Most probably the most obvious
part to study in that respect would be
\mathcal{H}_{\mathsf{C}}(\ast)
.

To provide further evidence that our method is robust, it is desirable to
apply it to axiomatise other separation logics, for instance
by adding the list segment predicate
\mathtt{ls}

[
BCO04
]

(or more generally user-defined inductive predicates)
or by adding first-order quantification. A key step in our approach is first to show that
the logic admits a characterisation in terms of core formulae and such formulae
need to be designed adequately. Of course, it is required that the set of valid formulae is recursively
enumerable, which discards any attempt with
\mathsf{SL(}\ast,\mathbin{-\mkern-6.0mu*},\mathtt{ls}\mathsf{)}
or with
the first-order version of
\mathsf{SL(}\ast,\mathbin{-\mkern-6.0mu*}\mathsf{)}

[
DLM18a
,
BDL12
]
.
The second part of the paper
[
DLM20
]
introduces an extension of
\mathsf{SL(}\ast,\mathtt{ls}\mathsf{)}

and presents an axiomatisation with our method. More separation logics could be axiomatised that way,
other good candidates are the version of separation logic with one individual variable
studied in
[
DGLWM17
]
as well as the quantifier-free separation logic with general universes
from
[
EIP19
]
.

We would like to thank the anonymous reviewers for their numerous
remarks and suggestions that help us to improve the quality of the document.

As in the rest of the paper, in the derivations below we use the following precedence between the various connectives of
\mathsf{SL(}\ast,\mathbin{-\mkern-6.0mu*}\mathsf{)}
:

\{\lnot\}>\{\land,\lor,\ast\}>\{\Rightarrow,\mathbin{-\mkern-6.0mu*},\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}\}>\{\Leftrightarrow\}
.

Appendix B
Derivation of the
\mathtt{size}
formulae required for Lemma
9

In this appendix, we show the derivations in
\mathcal{H}_{\mathsf{C}}(\ast)
of
\mathtt{size}\geq\beta_{1}+\beta_{2}\Rightarrow\mathtt{size}=\beta_{1}\ast\mathtt{size}\geq\beta_{2}
and
\mathtt{size}=\beta_{1}+\beta_{2}\Rightarrow\mathtt{size}=\beta_{1}\ast\mathtt{size}=\beta_{2}
, which are required for the proof of Lemma
9
.

The derivation of

\mathtt{size}\geq\beta_{1}+\beta_{2}\Rightarrow\mathtt{size}=\beta_{1}\ast\mathtt{size}\geq\beta_{2}

\beta_{1}

\beta_{1}=0
is:

Proof of

∗

13

)
.
The axiom
(

I
∗

13

)
is provable by
7
. Indeed, proving
(\perp\ast\varphi)\Rightarrow\perp

reduces to proving

\perp\Rightarrow(\varphi\mathbin{-\mkern-6.0mu*}\perp)
. The latter is a tautology by propositional reasoning. ∎

Proof D.9

(Proof of
(

I

-
⁣
∗

⁢

.23

)
)
.

Notice that, since
\varphi_{\mathtt{size}}
is satisfiable,
for every
\beta_{1},\beta_{2}\in\mathbb{N}
such that

\mathtt{size}\geq\beta_{1}\land\lnot\mathtt{size}\geq\beta_{2}\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\varphi_{\mathtt{size}}
,
we must have
\beta_{1}<\beta_{2}
.
Moreover, thanks to
(

I
C

9

)
and
(

I

-
⁣
∗

⁢

.7

)
,
without loss of generality, we can restrict ourselves to

\varphi_{\mathtt{size}}
of the form:

(1)

\varphi_{\mathtt{size}}=\mathtt{size}\geq\beta
for some
\beta\geq 0
,

(2)

\varphi_{\mathtt{size}}=\neg(\mathtt{size}\geq\beta)
for some
\beta>0
,

(3)

\varphi_{\mathtt{size}}=\mathtt{size}\geq\beta_{1}\wedge\neg(\mathtt{size}\geq\beta_{2})
for some
\beta_{2}>\beta_{1}
.

Indeed, given an arbitrary
\varphi_{\mathtt{size}}
,
every positive literal
\mathtt{size}\geq\beta
such that
\beta<\max_{\mathtt{size}}(\varphi_{\mathtt{size}})
can be derived starting from
\mathtt{size}\geq\max_{\mathtt{size}}(\varphi_{\mathtt{size}})
, by repeated applications of
(

I
C

9

)
.
Similarly, let
\overline{\beta}
be the smallest natural number
such that
\lnot\mathtt{size}\geq\overline{\beta}\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\varphi_{\mathtt{size}}
, if any.
Every literal
\lnot\mathtt{size}\geq\beta^{\prime}\subseteq_{\mathsf{Lt}}\varphi_{\mathtt{size}}
with
\beta^{\prime}\geq\overline{\beta}

can be derived from

\lnot\mathtt{size}\geq\overline{\beta}
, by repeated applications of the axiom
(

I
C

9

)
(taken in contrapositive form i.e.
\lnot\mathtt{size}\geq\beta\Rightarrow\lnot\mathtt{size}\geq\beta+1
, which is derivable in
\mathcal{H}_{\mathsf{C}}
by propositional reasoning).

We write
\textsf{\small{U}}(\mathtt{X})
to denote the conjunction

\bigwedge_{\mathtt{x}\in\mathtt{X}}\lnot\mathtt{alloc}(\mathtt{x})
(‘
U
’ stands for ‘unallocated’).
Below, given
\beta\in\mathbb{N}
,
we aim at deriving the formula
(\mathtt{size}=\beta\land\textsf{\small{U}}(\mathtt{X}))\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}\top

since this implies that
(

I

-
⁣
∗

⁢

.23

)
is derivable in
its instances
(1)
–
(3)
, as shown below.

case
(1)
:

Let
\varphi_{\mathtt{size}}=\mathtt{size}\geq\beta
.

1

\mathtt{size}=\beta\land\textsf{\small{U}}(\mathtt{X})\mathbin{{-\mkern-8.0mu{\raisebox{0.6pt}{$\scriptstyle{\circledast}$}}}}\top

induction step:

First of all, we notice that the following formula is valid:

(\mathtt{size}=1\land\textsf{\small{U}}(\mathtt{X}))\ast(\mathtt{size}=\beta{-}1\land\textsf{\small{U}}(\mathtt{X}))\Rightarrow\mathtt{size}=\beta\land\textsf{\small{U}}(\mathtt{X}).

(
\dagger
)

Indeed,
let
(s,h)
be a memory state satisfying the antecedent of the implication above.
So, there are disjoint heaps
h_{1}
and
h_{2}
such that

h=h_{1}{+}h_{2}
,
{\rm card}({\rm dom}(h_{1}))=1
,

{\rm card}({\rm dom}(h_{2}))=\beta-1
,
and for every
\mathtt{x}\in\mathtt{X}
,

s(\mathtt{x})\not\in{\rm dom}(h_{1})
and
s(\mathtt{x})\not\in{\rm dom}(h_{2})
.
By
h=h_{1}{+}h_{2}
,

{\rm card}({\rm dom}(h))={\rm card}({\rm dom}(h_{1}))+{\rm card}({\rm dom}(h_{2}))=\beta
, and for every
\mathtt{x}\in\mathtt{X}
,

s(\mathtt{x})\not\in{\rm dom}(h)
.
Thus,
(s,h)\models\mathtt{size}=\beta\land\textsf{\small{U}}(\mathtt{X})
.
